- **✓ NOT-A-BUG (investigated + closed 2026-05-31): backslash (`\`) line-continuation is intentionally absent — it was NEVER a Gorget feature.** The Chain 2 executor hit `expected expression, found NEWLINE` on a `\`-continued line and the output-review flagged it as a possible "gap"; investigation closed it: `\`-continuation is undocumented, unimplemented, and used by ZERO `.gg` files. Gorget's DELIBERATE multi-line mechanisms are (1) **bracket suppression** — `()`/`[]`/`{}` suppress newline+indent (`language-reference.md:44`), and (2) **leading-dot continuation** for method chains (`src/lexer/mod.rs:141-146`, `devbook/03-lexer.md:79`). The lexer's `\` handling is string-escapes only (`src/lexer/mod.rs:482-538`), by design. So the idiomatic multi-line predicate is parenthesizing (`(a or\n b)`), NOT `\`. No action — logged only so it isn't re-investigated as a snag. (Adding `\`-continuation would be redundant feature-creep against the language design.)

- **🧩 CoW MATERIALIZATION — durable reference (THE RULE + GROUND TRUTH below) + open follow-ups.** The PERF audit's concrete follow-ups are in the "🔁 ROUND R1 FOLLOW-UPS" item directly below. (The 4-brief/parallel-plan prose further down is older cruft from the round that executed this — prune when convenient.)

  **THE RULE (owner-decided 2026-05-30, governs ALL materialization work):** *Never implicitly clone except when absolutely necessary for safety.* Preference order **borrow > move > clone**. An implicit clone is legitimate ONLY when BOTH hold: (1) the destination must OWN the value (a real ownership boundary), AND (2) the source can be neither aliased (borrow would dangle / alias-mutate) nor moved (source stays live past this point, OR is a collection element / `.get()` result / view-returning-method result / parameter — those can't be moved out). Any clone NOT forced by (1)∧(2) is a bug. `.clone()` = explicit eager owned copy; `!` = move. **Explicit-type bindings borrow** (`Vector[int] row = matrix[0]`, `String x = obj.field` behave identically to `auto`) — NO auto-clone on read/explicit-type; the clone defers to the later mutation (CoW-sever) or escape (ownership boundary). For collection-element/view/param sources, ownership-taking is **clone-NOT-move** (the source retains its element). This IS the existing `CLAUDE.md` "Ownership at Consuming Positions" contract.

  **GROUND TRUTH (verified vs source this session — re-cite before acting):**
  - **Enforcement / safety net:** `validate_consume_sites` (`src/ir/validate.rs:2400`) runs UNCONDITIONALLY in `lower_module` (`src/ir/lowering/mod.rs:1892`) and `panic!`s (`:1977`) on the FIRST owning position (StructInit/EnumInit/TupleInit/Call-ByValue/CallExtern/CollectionMutator/BoxNew/AssignIntoOwnedSlot) that receives a borrow with no preceding clone/move; every class fatal (2026-05-10). ⇒ a MISSING materialization CRASHES the compiler, doesn't miscompile. ANY clone-removal must keep this GREEN.
  - **Taxonomy:** `ImplicitCloneReason` (`src/ir/mod.rs:183`) — 11 variants, 39 emission sites, via 3 helpers: `ensure_owned_at_boundary` (`context.rs:1752` — return/struct-field/tuple/match-escape/closure-capture), `ensure_owned_at_consuming_arg` (`context.rs:1923` — collection-consume/index-assign/field-store/Box; last-use-aware move-or-clone), `emit_enum_init_owned` (`context.rs:1243` — enum-variant payload) — plus WRITE-side `cow_before_mutation` (`context.rs:2658`, 6 cases) and a **GorgetString-ONLY** runtime materialize net (every non-string resource relies entirely on the compile-time clone — why the validator must be exact).
  - **Var-decl tree** `lower_var_decl_assign_mode` (`src/ir/lowering/stmts/mod.rs:1081-1273`): A/C → Borrow (CoW alias), F/G → Move (dead source), **B/D/E → Clone** (B = cross-type live `Str→GorgetString`; D = CoW-unsafe live; E = `View`→owned String). **B & E are the over-clone SUSPECTS** — a read-only binding from a view/cross-type source should stay a borrow under THE RULE; verify before trusting.
  - **CLEAN REFERENCES (settle everything else TO these):** `CLAUDE.md` "Ownership at Consuming Positions" (re-verified clean) + `docs/book/11-ownership.md` §"auto vs Explicit Type" (`:282-300` — "auto and explicit type behave the same — both borrow"; `.clone()` for owned) + `language-reference.md:2321`/`:1624` (already correct).

Needs a coordinated migration of those consumers (or a separate `is_resource_or_enum_payload` predicate threaded only to the two deref-store gates), not a one-line change. `has_resource_fields` (`types.rs:703`) already has the right enum-descending shape but isn't interchangeable (downstream branches on different axes).   - **🐛 Self-host lowerer driver OMITS a borrow-check pass (scout-confirmed 2026-05-31 agent af99281f).** The self-host `driver.gg` pipeline is parse→resolve→typecheck→lower→validators — NO borrow-check pass. So a move-then-use of a named local (e.g. bare `Some(name)` then `nl_put(name)` — a value moved into an owned consuming position while still live) that Rust gg correctly REJECTS at borrow-check (`use of moved value`) is instead SILENTLY lowered by the self-host to a leaking MOVE (LSan-confirmed leak; latent use-after-free), with no diagnostic. The CoW *lowering* is correct (move-by-default at consuming positions, matching Rust — the `Some(name.clone())` idiom at `lower.gg:5900/5969/5977` is the language-MANDATED explicit clone, NOT a workaround, and must STAY). The gap is purely the MISSING DIAGNOSTIC GATE: the self-host should run the borrow checker so misuse errors instead of leaking. Fix = port/wire a borrow-check pass into the self-host driver pipeline (the resolver dir has diagnostic infra; the lowerer driver doesn't run it). Real self-host correctness-gap (it only bites invalid programs / produces leaks, so lower urgency than a miscompile, but it's a genuine "self-host should catch this" hole). NOTE the related language-design observation: collection consuming positions (`push`/`put`) AUTO-clone a live source, but a bare named-local into an enum/struct payload requires an explicit `.clone()`/`!` — confirm that asymmetry is intended (it matches Rust gg) before any "make Some() auto-clone too" change.

Mitigated here by the env-gate (off by default) + always running the measurement on the Rust-gg-built driver, but the underlying `file=stderr` lowering hole is real. WRITER site: the `print`-call lowering in `lower.gg` doesn't model the `file=` kwarg. Fix would let self-host-built drivers emit diagnostics to stderr like Rust gg.

- **(Medium, CORRECTNESS — discovered 2026-05-29 during Bug-B Stage-1 fixture work) Index-load `STATIC_VEC[i].field` on a static `Vector[struct]` returns a ZEROED struct.** With Bug-B Stage 1 landed (static collection literals now materialize correctly), `STATIC_VEC[i].field` reads `0`/empty for every field, while `STATIC_VEC.get(i).unwrap().field` reads the correct value, AND `[i]` indexing on a *local* `Vector[struct]` works fine. So the elements ARE populated (Bug B is fixed) — this is a SEPARATE bug in the `IndexLoad`/by-value-element-read path specifically for *static* (global) struct vectors: the index-load against a global slot produces a zeroed temp rather than loading the stored element. Repro: `public static Vector[Item] T = [Item("a",1)]` + `print(T[0].name)` → empty; `T.get(0).unwrap().name` → `"a"`. Likely a backend codegen issue distinguishing global vs local collection base in the index-load lowering. `tests/fixtures/static_vec_literal.gg` uses `.get(i).unwrap()` to sidestep it; the `#[ignore]`'d `static_vec_index_load` test (`tests/fixtures/static_vec_index_load.gg`) asserts the CORRECT `[i].field` behavior — flip it to active once fixed.

- **(Low/Medium, CORRECTNESS — pre-existing, surfaced 2026-05-29 in Bug-B Stage-1 output-review) Static-decl initializers are NOT type-checked against their declared type the way locals are.** `public static int X = [1,2,3]` is accepted by `gg check` ("OK"), whereas the equivalent LOCAL `int X = [1,2,3]` is correctly rejected (`type mismatch`). Pre-existing hole in static-decl semantic analysis (it validates local var-decl init-vs-declared-type but not static-decl). Mostly latent (only mis-typed programs hit it), BUT Bug-B Stage 1 changed its SYMPTOM for collection-literal RHS: `initializer_needs_synthetic_fn` keys on RHS shape only (`Expr::ArrayLiteral`/`DictLiteral`), so a type-mismatched `static int X = [1,2,3]` now flows into the synthetic init fn → garbage pointer + C warning (`returning 'void *' from a fn with return type 'int64_t'`), where before it silently printed `0` (Zeroed). Neither is correct; the real fix is adding static-decl initializer type-checking in semantic analysis (mirror the local var-decl check). Only affects already-malformed programs.

The variant-collision fix (#1) is independently useful (any self-host program with a variant name shared across 2 enums) — could land STANDALONE with the bare-`TArray` exposing fixture `tests/fixtures/variant_name_collision.gg` (in branch `…a735fb0a…`). **BuiltinMethodDecl port stays BLOCKED** until Stage 2 fully lands (held `81d654db` + `docs/plans/artifacts/builtin_method_decl_step2_consumer.patch`). - **(Low) Bootstrap stage-1 timeout default (120s) too short for the slow large-file self-host compiles [re-confirmed 2026-05-29].** `self_host_bootstrap`'s `GG_STAGE1_TIMEOUT_SECS` default (120) is blown by the slow lower.gg/driver.gg/lir_lower.gg compiles (~100-500s) — runs need it bumped to 900-1200. Pre-existing (baseline e9236f96 equally slow), matches the test's own "flaky under load" comment. NOTE: `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` does NOT honor this knob (hardcoded 600s/stage). Tied to the 4GB-RSS compile-slowness (task #16) — fixing the clone-bomb should bring compile times down under the defaults.

- **🚨 (High, fidelity — c_emit is the BIGGEST parity gap; the 7-crash cluster is now FIXED, CLASS 1 is the dominant remaining target).** Counts (post the 7-crash fix, `e281dd47`): **717 matched, 310 mismatched, 0 self-host crashes** (was 716/302/7 — the 7-crash cluster ELIMINATED → DONE.md; those fixtures now emit C and moved into the mismatch pool, mostly CLASS-1 territory); +92 rust-crashes. "matched" = `user_fn_count` parity only, NOT byte-identical C (true byte-parity is lower). Remaining target:
  - **CLASS 1 (DOMINANT, ~150+ fixtures): trait-impl method bodies dropped by self-host DCE.** The self-host `compute_reachable_fns` (`lir_codegen.gg:918`, transitive walk `:1052-1061`) lacks two seeding edges Rust's `find_live_functions` (`src/lir/optimize.rs:183`) has: (i) transitive `IFuncAddr` (mirror the existing `IClosurePack` arm), (ii) scan `m.globals` initializers for FuncAddr/vtable roots (`optimize.rs:262-268`; can reuse `collect_func_addr_targets` at `lir_codegen.gg:1486`). ⚠ CONFIRM-FIRST (CLAUDE.md debugging heuristic — trace the missing edge to its WRITE site): the self-host LIR for json_parse shows ZERO IFuncAddr/vtable-global edges to these methods, so the gap may be UPSTREAM (the self-host trait-vtable lowering ~`lower.gg:9686-9750` doesn't emit the FuncAddr-laden vtable globals Rust does). Run `driver json_parse.gg lib --emit-lir | grep -i 'global\|FuncAddr'` to decide DCE-seeding-only vs vtable-emission + DCE-seeding. Rep: json_parse (rust=67 gg=28; misses all Serializer/Deserializer trait methods + the io.gg Writer/Reader cluster shared across 62 fixtures).
  - CLASS 2 (~24 fixtures): httpserver TLS/keep-alive variant fns (`*_tls*`/`*_ka*`) — monomorphization-variant emission diff. CLASS 3: small one-offs (catch/throws, struct-by-value, async-wrapper, iterator-mono, spurious extra DataFrame__drop). Full diagnosis: agent a3a14f53 (2026-05-29).

- **🚨 (High, perf — MEMORY BOMB: ~1.5B array_clones / 1.18GB peak RSS; root = per-instruction CoW consuming-position clones) [attribution measured 2026-05-29 pm₂; baseline at tip `168e988e`: array_clone 1,500,904,440] — ⚠ SUPERSEDED by the ✅ entry above; the bomb was `get_fn_param_types` value-bind, not these lir_ssa rebuild sites. Kept only for the mechanism notes; the open hypotheses here are CLOSED.** Mechanism: `gorget_array_push` does NOT itself deep-copy — the clone is emitted at the CoW *consuming position* (pushing a BORROW read from the old vector into `new_insts` forces a clone-before-call; you cannot move out of a borrowed collection element). So the cure is to stop rebuilding and mutate in place. `read_variable`'s prepend-rebuild (now at the entry-block zero-init branch, was ~:255) was converted to in-place `f.blocks.get(bb).unwrap().insts.insert(0, const)` — and this needed **NO new primitive** (`insert(0,…)` IS insert-front via the already-wired `gorget_array_insert`; the const is a fresh owned temp that moves in, existing elems memmove). Measured delta (`scripts/self_host_mem_baseline.sh`): array_clone 1,502,811,719 → 1,500,904,440 = **−1.9M (−0.1%)** — read_variable's zero-init is a MINOR path (fires only for entry-block undefined promotable slots). ✅ landed (see DONE.md, output-neutral: fixed_point GREEN 644s, lowerer_comparison count-identical). **So the remaining ~1.5B is concentrated in the OTHER two sites, which rebuild on EVERY instruction of EVERY block:** `process_block` (filter-rebuild ~:203 — drops promoted slot-store/load insts, deep-clones every KEPT inst into `new_insts`) and `apply_value_substitutions` (rewrite ~:329 — clones at `substitute_inst`'s by-value arg for every inst). **⚠ LOCATION UNKNOWN — TWO measured guesses came back tiny, so STOP guessing and do ATTRIBUTION-FIRST:** `read_variable` in-place insert = **−1.9M (−0.1%)** (shipped 168e988e); `emit()` chained-in-place = **−419K (−0.0%)** (measured + reverted — the compiler already lowers that get-mutate-set efficiently; its "can't push to nested vector" comment IS confirmed-stale → keep as a SHOWCASE-CLEANUP candidate, one-line `ctx.block_insts.get(ctx.current_block).unwrap().push(inst)`, validate via fixed_point, but NOT perf-motivated). At ~2250 clones per output C line the bomb is either (a) one massively-concentrated/quadratic site not yet measured, or (b) **distributed by design** — the DONE-trail apples-to-apples gate found current gg's CoW emission is CLEAN (byte-identical fixtures clone ~0) and attributed the 1.5B to self-host SOURCE clone-density + 1.5× workload growth, which is consistent with no-single-site-dominates. **NEXT perf step = MEASURE, not convert:** add a C accessor for `__gorget_array_clone_count` (`c_runtime.rs:45`) + a Gorget extern (mirror `gorget_mem_live` in `lib/std/os.gg:15`) + instrument `driver.gg` main() to print the array_clone delta per pipeline phase (parse/resolve/typecheck/lower/ssa/codegen) → localize the dominant phase, THEN the hot site, BEFORE any fix. If it's distributed, it's a self-host `.clone()`-density audit, not a single-primitive fix. **Site A `apply_value_substitutions`** (perf-design's UNMEASURED "dominant" hypothesis: substitute_inst by-VALUE param clones each inst; fix = `substitute_inst_inplace(LirInst &inst,…)`, both crux capabilities probe-verified, DISJOINT lir_ssa.gg) and **Site B `process_block`** (filter; pre-pass hoist + `remove_at`→existing `gorget_array_remove`) — design at `docs/plans/lir_ssa_inplace_primitive_design.md` — are now CANDIDATES TO MEASURE, not confirmed targets (given the 2 surprises, measure Site A's delta before trusting the design). Deferred (not the bomb): drop_elab outer set-back `m.functions.set(fi,f)` is load-bearing (correct CoW).

- **(Medium, perf) f-string per-key `malloc` still 1.6–3× slower than Python on Vector/Dict insert benches [added 2026-05-29, deferred from R2 perf chain].** The string-formatting half is fixed (single-pass `gorget_string_format`, commit `19266913`), but `Vector[String] push fstring x100` (0.60× Python) and `Dict[Str,int] put fstring key x50` (0.37× Python) remain slow — the residual is the per-key heap `malloc` + the Dict/Vector INSERT path, NOT the formatting. Deeper fix is a larger change: arena-backed key interning for Dict puts, or pre-sizing the inserted buffer so the f-string result lands directly in the collection's storage without a throwaway heap alloc. Lazy-iterator framing may apply to the insert path. Separate, larger chain than the formatting micro-opt.

- **(Low, perf/correctness-note) `.len()` on MALFORMED UTF-8 changed behavior with the SWAR rewrite [added 2026-05-29, commit `19266913`].** The new `gorget_str_codepoint_count` computes `len − continuation_bytes` (= count of lead bytes); the old per-byte walk counted a stray continuation byte at a lead position AS a codepoint (e.g. `"\x80\x80\x80"`: old=3, new=0). On WELL-FORMED UTF-8 both are identical (verified 66/66 + multibyte/ZWJ/alignment cases). `.len()` on ill-formed UTF-8 is unspecified and Gorget strings are expected well-formed (`gorget_string_is_valid_utf8` exists for validation), so this is acceptable + arguably more defensible. Only actionable IF a strict-on-invalid-input `.len()` contract is ever required.

- **(Medium) `drop_elaboration` super-linear: 5.6× per-instruction cost inflation on large fns [added 2026-05-28, profiling pass #1 §Hotspot 2].** `src/lir/drop_elab.rs:forward_dataflow` (`:105-176`), `meet_states` (`:239-247`), `elaborate_drops` (`:712`). drop_elab takes 133ms / 18% of TOTAL compile on self_host_lowerer — the single biggest fraction in one named sub-pass. Per-instruction cost scaling: yaml_parse 109ns → typechecker 204ns → lowerer 606ns (5.6×). Cost shape: `O(n_blocks × n_slots × #iters_to_fixpoint)`; per-block worklist dataflow clones a dense `Vec<InitState>` of size `n_slots` at every node-pop + every successor edge. Fix: switch `SlotStates` from `Vec<InitState>` to packed bit-vector (`[u64; (n_slots+31)/32]` — 8× cache density, branch-free meet); avoid per-edge `out.clone()` if predecessor saturated; early-exit on saturation (most fns converge in ≤2 iters). Success metric: drop_elab on self_host_lowerer drops 133ms → ≤40ms (3.3×), total compile -12%. Verification gates: byte-identical drop-elab output on fixture corpus + full integration suite.

- **(Medium) Dict/Set/String runtime perf SLOWER than Python (0.23-0.52× ratio) [added 2026-05-28, profiling pass #1 §Hotspot 3].** Dict[String,int] build+lookup 0.48×, Dict iterate 0.39×, Set[int] 0.44×, Set[String] 0.52×, string-len-x32-loop 0.23×, string-slice-hot 0.33×, string-concat 0.61×, Vector[String]-fstring 0.47×. Root: `src/backend/c/c_runtime.rs` (a) FNV-1a default hash (`__gorget_fnv1a` `:226`) vs CPython's SipHash + per-cell hot path; (b) `%` on non-power-of-two cap is a 20-cycle divide vs CPython's bit-mask on power-of-two; (c) function-pointer indirection `hash_fn`/`eq_fn` (`:5753-5754`) blocks inlining for int/string hot paths; (d) every put incurs allocator calls for the order vector. Fix: swap to wyhash or rapidhash; constrain `m->cap` to power-of-two + replace `% cap` with `& (cap-1)`; specialize `__gorget_map_put_int` / `_str` to skip fn-ptr dispatch. Success metric: Dict[String,int] build+lookup 18.5µs → ≤6µs (≥3× Python); Set[int] 9.4µs → ≤3µs. String-in-loop part is separate sub-investigation (suspected: `.len()` and slice are allocating per-iter inside hot loops; `string len access` standalone 19ns but 90ns/iter in a 32-loop = 22× cost inflation).

- **(Medium) Vec2 dot product 13.7× slower than Rust; numerical benches 5-14× slower than Rust across the board [added 2026-05-28, profiling pass #1 §Hotspot 5].** Operational target: within 2× of Rust on numerical benches. All 5 compute benches FAIL: fib(20) 5.6×, for-range 7.5×, Vec2 dot 13.7×, while-10000 7.5×, nested-100×100 9.6×. Worst: Vec2 dot — hints at struct-by-value ABI overhead (passing 16-byte struct through memory not registers, or clone+drop at call boundary; Rust's `#[derive(Copy)]` lets LLVM register-pass two f64s). Recurring `while i < N` loops at 2.49 ns/iter vs Rust 0.33 — drop-flag checks or missed loop-strength reduction in our C output. Diagnosis: disassemble both binaries with `objdump -d`, tabulate per-call instruction count. Fix locus: `src/backend/c_lir/` (ABI emission) or `src/lir/lower/` (struct-by-value lowering). Success metric: Vec2 dot ≤200 ns/iter (within 2.5× Rust); fib(20) ≤25µs.

- **(Medium) Codegen 2× per-instruction inflation on large workloads [added 2026-05-28, profiling pass #1 §Hotspot 4].** `src/backend/c_lir/mod.rs` (3178 lines), `helpers.rs` (1990 lines). 132ms / 17.5% of total compile on self_host_lowerer; scaling 306 → 602 ns/inst (yaml_parse → lowerer). Same shape as drop_elab inflation. **Needs sub-pass instrumentation BEFORE picking a fix** — mirror `gir_lower`'s timing depth (per-fn forward-decl, per-fn body, per-block, per-inst). Once data is in hand, fix locus is TBD. Success metric: codegen ≤60ms on self_host_lowerer (≥2× speedup), total compile -9%.

- **(Medium) `Some` first-arg-non-primitive `ret_type` backstop in `lower_call` is NOT subsumed by the principled `ctx.expected_type` path [updated 2026-05-28 pm₃ — partial-completion of the backstop sweep; site 1 SHIPPED, site 2 RESERVED with concrete fault data].** Sweep finding: the SReturn after-the-fact retype + `expr_is_prelude_variant_ctor` helper retired cleanly (commit `90c947d3`, see DONE.md 2026-05-28 — site 1 of the originally-planned two-site sweep). The `Some` first-arg block at the bottom of `lower_call`'s ret_type chain (NOW at `lower.gg:~5693-5723`, with the dead `int first_arg_type = -1` local at `:5574` + write at `:5594-5595`) was attempted in a separate cycle and **regressed `self_host_bootstrap`**: stage-1 cc fails with multiple `expected 'void *' but argument is of type 'Option__GorgetString'` errors at `lower___add_local`, `lower___add_local_with`, `lower___add_local_inheriting`, and `lower___bind_for_local` (caller-side has `Option__String`; callee param signature emitted as `void *`). The principled `expected_type` path at the TOP of `lower_call` (`:5644-5653`) requires the per-arg propagation at STEP 2.3 (`:5586-5593`) to set `ctx.expected_type = pt` from `get_fn_param_types(call_name)`. For self-host calls like `add_local(&ctx, T, Some(name))` where `add_local`'s third param is `Option[String]`, this SHOULD route. The regression suggests something in that chain isn't firing for these specific call sites — possibly `get_fn_param_types` returning empty/wrong types for some monomorphised callees, or a separate path (param-signature emission, not ret_type resolution) was actually depending on the `Some` block's per-call-site result-typing side-effect. **Concrete fault sites** (from the failing /tmp/self_host_stage1.c diff): callers in `lower___lower_stmt`, `lower___bind_for_local`, `lower___lower_function`, `lower___lower_equip_block`. **Diagnostic priority next**: instrument the principled path's hit/miss for these specific `add_local{,_with,_inheriting}(&ctx, T, Some(name))` calls in driver.gg; confirm whether `get_fn_param_types("add_local")` returns the right `Option__String` type_id, and whether `pt != I64_TYPE and pt != UNIT_TYPE` is true for that pt. Until the actual subsuming mechanism is identified and the gap closed, **the `Some` first-arg block + `first_arg_type` local STAY**. Site 2 reverted (working tree clean at `90c947d3`).

- **bug #3b STATUS (2026-05-27 pm) — clone-OOM CLEARED + large-String truncation FIXED (commit `4ce2be0f`; see DONE.md). `self_host_bootstrap` PASSES.** Truncation root cause was a self-host lowering fidelity gap: view-returning String methods (`.slice()`/`.trim()`/`.strip()`/`.substring()`/`.str()`/...) had their result local tagged `LoOwned` instead of `LoView` in `lower.gg`, so `op_consume` MOVED the cap==0 view into owned storage instead of cloning it → dangling view → NUL bytes (`sizeof(\0\0\0\0)`) in the ~15 MB `generate_c` output → `print`'s `%s`/`%.*s` (both stop at NUL) truncated at 1,633,116 B. Fix: `is_string_view_method` predicate + tag results `LoView()` at the method-call result site (mirrors Rust `returns_view: true`). s1bin now emits the FULL 663,687-line / 14.9 MB stage-2, ZERO NULs. `cargo test --lib --release` 1060/1062 (pre-existing floor).

- **(Low, self-host showcase cleanup) Retire the byte-by-byte Box-inner-name extraction loops at `lower.gg:5035` (EFieldAccess box-deref) and `lower.gg:5340` (EDeref `*box`) IF the cited `.substring()`/`.slice()`-returns-empty view-aliasing bug is gone [added 2026-05-28 pm₄, alongside NEXT BLOCKER #6 close].** Both loops carry comments claiming `dsrc_tname.substring(5, len)` / `.slice(5, len)` "returns empty" for Box-prefixed type names due to "view-aliasing", so they walk bytes via `chr(byte_at(i))` instead. With NEXT BLOCKER #6's `chr`→GorgetString fix, the byte loop now WORKS, but the underlying `.substring()` view-aliasing claim is unverified-and-possibly-stale (it predates the `is_string_view_method` LoView fix of bug #3b, commit `4ce2be0f`). Per the self-host-as-showcase rule: verify whether `name.substring(5, name.len())` still returns empty in a stage-2 binary; if it's fixed, replace both byte loops with the idiomatic `.substring()`/`.slice()` call. If still broken, that's a SEPARATE live view-aliasing bug to file. Output-neutral either way (same inner name extracted) — verify via `bootstrap_fixed_point`.

- **(Low, self-host showcase cleanup) Retire the stale "memset bug" comment + get-mutate-set shape in `scope.gg` `set_def_param`/`set_def_type` [added 2026-05-30, runner-up from the `__`-scan cleanup round].** `set_def_param` (in `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/scope.gg`, ~`:271`) carries `# Can't use get_def_at in &self (memset bug), so reconstruct` and does a `get(id).unwrap()` → mutate-local → `set(id, d)` dance to dodge a bug that is DEAD: `define_replace_or_fail` (same file, ~`:142`) already calls `get_def_at(self.definitions, existing_def_id)` from `&self` successfully. The comment is a false-historical record. NOT fixed in this round because retiring the get-mutate-set shape is a SEMANTIC change (in-place field mutation vs reconstruct), NOT output-neutral — needs its own `fixed_point`-gated validation. When picked up: confirm the bug is still dead, replace the reconstruct with direct in-place mutation, drop the comment, and verify via `bootstrap_fixed_point` + `lowerer_comparison` count-identical. The sibling `set_def_type` (and any other `# ...(memset bug)...` reconstruct in the same file) shares the smell — sweep them together.

- **Self-host lowerer: `Option[Ref]` return not auto-deref+cloned when declared return is `Option[value]` [added 2026-05-27, deeper gap behind Option[Ref] bootstrap layer 6].** The self-host lowerer does NOT materialize (deref+clone) an `Option[Ref[T]]` payload at a `return` whose declared type is `Option[T]` (a value). Rust's lowerer does — that's why `driver` was correct but `s1bin` mis-lowered. Surfaced in `resource_meta_for` (`lir_lower.gg:~416`): `return gmod.resource_metadata.get(name)` returned a raw `Option[Ref[ResourceMetadata]]`; the un-deref'd Ref read as None in s1bin → `is_resource_type_name` falsely false → `op_consume` emitted `OpCopy` on resources → 1238 FATAL `validate_resource_moves` violations. **Worked around** for the bootstrap with an explicit `Some(...get(name).unwrap().clone())` one-liner (idiomatic — a value-returning fn should materialize). DEEPER fix: make the lowerer auto-deref+clone any `Option[Ref]`/`Ref` return coerced to a value-typed return (mirroring Rust); then the explicit `.clone()` workaround can be retired and sibling `return <dict>.get(k)` shapes are covered automatically. See `docs/plans/option_ref_borrow.md` Layer 6.

- **(Low) Self-host loader `find_project_root_for` is cwd-fragile [added 2026-05-27].** `load_imports`'s project-root fallback for `compiler.data.*` imports (`loader.gg:~596`, `find_project_root_for(base_dir, root_seg)`) walks UP from `base_dir`, which is the raw entry-file path. When the driver is invoked with a *relative* entry (e.g. `driver driver.gg lib` from inside the fixture dir), `base_dir`'s parent resolves to `"."`, so the upward walk can only ever find `./compiler` — it fails unless cwd is at/under the repo root. The canonical `self_host_bootstrap` test passes ABSOLUTE paths from repo-root cwd, so it's unaffected; this only bites ad-hoc invocations. Fix: anchor the walk on the entry file's ABSOLUTE parent dir (canonicalize `path` first), mirroring `src/loader.rs`. Discovered when a hand-rolled bootstrap cycle script (run from the fixture dir) caused `schema.gg` to silently not load → 35 spurious `unknown field … ResourceMetadata` + bare `(Option*)` cc errors — a pure harness artifact, NOT a compiler bug. Lesson also logged: validate the bootstrap via `cargo test self_host_bootstrap[_fixed_point]`, never a hand-rolled script.

- **SELF-HOST lexer bug (NOT a Rust bug): string literal inside an f-string interpolation truncates the enclosing `match` [added: 2026-05-24; RE-ATTRIBUTED 2026-05-27].** A nested string literal in an f-string interpolation segment — e.g. `f"... {overflow_op(\"add\", x)} ..."` — is mis-lexed: the `\"` inside `{...}` ends the f-string early, so everything after that `case` arm is silently dropped from the parse. Discovered while adding `format_lir.gg` (the `--emit-lir` formatter): the `IAdd` arm used `overflow_op(\"add\", overflow)` inline; the whole `match inst:` then reported `non-exhaustive match: missing variants: ISub, IMul, …` (every arm after `IAdd`), and the function was silently lowered to an auto-registered variadic extern (linker error `undefined reference`). **Failure mode is nasty**: no parse error at the interpolation site, just a downstream non-exhaustive-match error one function over, plus a phantom extern. Repro: any `case` arm whose body is `return f"...{f(\"lit\", x)}..."` in a non-final match arm. Workaround used in `format_lir.gg`: bind the inner call to a `String` local first, then interpolate the local. **CORRECTION (verified 2026-05-27): the Rust compiler does NOT have this bug** — `src/lexer/mod.rs:691-694` (the `{`-scan loop in `scan_string_literal`) already calls `skip_quoted_string` on a nested `"`/`'`, and `skip_quoted_string` (`mod.rs:922-935`) skips `\\` escapes, so `\"` inside `{...}` is handled correctly. Confirmed by direct test: a two-arm `match` whose arms `return f"...{g(\"lit\", x)}..."` lowers and runs correctly under `gg run`. The bug is exclusively in the **self-host lexer fixture** (`tests/fixtures/self_host_*/lex.gg` — the interpolation-segment scanner there must mirror `skip_quoted_string`'s escape handling). Fix belongs to the self-host modernization track, NOT `src/lexer/`. Do not chase a phantom Rust lexer bug.

- **`std.slotmap` shipped (2026-05-25); resource-`T` leak FIXED + ECS reimpl DONE (both 2026-05-25, see DONE.md).** Generational-handle arena (`SlotMap[T]` + `SlotKey`) at `lib/std/slotmap.gg`, registered in `src/stdlib.rs`, wired into `integration.rs` (`slotmap_basic`, `slotmap_stale_key`, and now `slotmap_cycle_no_leak` all pass — the last un-`#[ignore]`'d once the consuming-position clone leak was fixed). The 4 `weak_cycle_*` fixtures + tests were deleted (abandoned Weak/Cell approach, superseded). NOTE: `SlotMap` had to be its own module — putting it in `collections.gg` broke `iter_chain_past_one_step` (that file is builtin-stub-only; real impl code there interacts badly with iterator monomorphization). That collections.gg-pollution interaction is itself an unexplained latent smell.

- **(Deferred, Low) Reimplement `EntityPool` atop a value-less `SlotMap`.** Optional follow-up to the 2026-05-25 ECS-onto-SlotKey unification (DONE.md). `EntityPool` (free-list + generations key authority in `lib/xtd/ecs.gg`) duplicates `SlotMap`'s allocation algorithm; a value-less `SlotMap` could share it. No blocker — `Entity` is already `type Entity = SlotKey` and `SparseSet[T]` stays a deliberately-dense component store (do NOT fold into SlotMap).

If the drop emission plan blows past 60h, this is the rollback target — `git reset --hard f15a45c6` returns to "labels-only self-host that bootstraps cleanly" at the cost of all post-A.2 architectural progress (today's lex_emit fix + forward-decl prep would need re-applying on top).

- **Path A driver.gg OOM diagnosed 2026-05-23 — root cause: elaborate_drops leaks ~25-30 MB/function (~13 GB total).** With the lex_emit clone-after-push fix landed today, parser.gg / loader.gg / traits.gg compile cleanly. driver.gg still OOM-killed. Diagnostic narrowed the leak to a SPECIFIC pass:

  Per-pipeline-step memory deltas (instrumented driver.gg main()):
    parse_source:        +462 KB
    load_imports:        +174 MB
    expand_meta_for_match: +53 MB
    resolve_module:      +440 MB
    type_check_module:   +470 MB
    lower_module:        +1.06 GB
    run_validators:      +4 MB
    lower_gir_to_lir:    +95 MB
    run_ssa:             +157 MB
    **elaborate_drops:   +10.76 GB**  ← THE OOM SOURCE
    validate_lir_after:  +0 KB (no-op without GG_VALIDATE_PASSES)
    generate_c:          +213 MB (was the previous suspect — NOT the culprit)

  Per-iteration breakdown inside elaborate_drops (50-fn batches):
    batches 0-200:   slow growth, ~3 MB/fn
    batches 200-450: spikes to 40-80 MB/fn, totaling ~10 GB
    Function 262 (tiny: 4 blocks, 50 insts, 20 slots) showed a 3.5 GB jump at its iteration boundary — leak is NOT proportional to function size; accumulates from prior iterations and surfaces at allocator-page boundaries.

  **Root cause class**: self-host doesn't emit drops on slot reassignment inside loops. Each iteration of the `while fi < m.functions.len()` body in elaborate_drops re-assigns slots holding `Dict[int, int]`, `Vector[Vector[int]]`, `LirFunction`, etc. without dropping the prior content. The runtime's `gorget_array_set` etc. DO have drop-on-overwrite via `elem_drop` fn pointers — but stage1.c has **0** `LirFunction__drop` / `<UserType>__drop` symbols emitted, and `gorget_array_new_drop(elem_size, drop_fn)` is only called 1-2 times in the entire ~600 KB stage1.c. The typed elem_drop function pointers aren't being threaded through Vector[T] constructions for user types.

  **Tried**: explicit `.clear()` on per-iter locals (val_to_slot, in_states, in_states_present, deleted_slots, maybe_init_slots, bb0_state) at end of iteration. **Didn't help** — memory still grew to 13.2 GB. The leak is deeper than the visible local-overwrite sites; likely propagates through f.blocks.set / nested resource-containing structs whose drop chains aren't emitted.

  **Note on agent vs parent**: the agent worktree's self_host_bootstrap PASSED at 211s. Parent's bootstrap FAILS at 1170s. The difference is timing-fragile — auto-scaled GG_STAGE1_TIMEOUT_SECS and system load determine whether stage-1 OOMs before timeout. Don't trust a single PASS.

  **Three options for unblocking driver.gg** (recommended ordering):
    A) **Targeted: emit_struct_drops + gorget_array_new_drop registration**. Self-host's `emit_struct_drops` already generates `<Type>__drop` C code, BUT they aren't being registered as elem_drop on Vector[Type] constructions. Add a post-pass in `lir_lower.gg` or `lir_codegen.gg` that, for every Vector[T] / Dict[K,V] construction of a user type, emits `gorget_array_new_drop(sizeof(T), T__drop)` instead of bare `gorget_array_new`. Smaller scope than full Phase 4; reuses existing drop emission. **Recommended start point.**
    B) **Ship full Phase 4 / E.1** (drop emission + lower_return MoveZero). Bigger lift; this is the keystone the Rust machinery port has been building toward.
    C) **Refactor elaborate_drops** to mutate in place via & instead of get-modify-set. Pragmatic band-aid; violates the "self-host as elegance showcase" principle.

  **Suggested diagnostic methodology** (worked today, document for future): added `gorget_diag_n(int n)` runtime helper that prints `[diag] checkpoint N (live=NN KB)` to stderr (uses existing `gorget_mem_live()` counters). Instrument driver.gg main + suspected pass entry points; rerun manually with `lib_dir = lib` (NOT the self-host directory — that's the lib_dir gotcha documented below). The function-batch granularity (`if fns_processed % 50 == 0: gorget_diag_n(...)`) catches the dominant offender quickly.

- **`tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/driver.gg` is NOT the lib_dir.** Subtle gotcha from today's debugging: manual `stage1 <input.gg> <lib_dir> --lir-c` invocations must use `lib` (or absolute `$(pwd)/lib`) as `lib_dir`. Using `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer` makes the loader silently skip std.fs / std.os (no `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/std/` directory exists), `call_redirects` never populates for `getenv` / `write_file` / `is_dir` / `append_file` / etc., and stage-1 emits the bare Gorget names which fail cc with "incompatible type for argument 1 of getenv". The integration test uses `manifest_dir.join("lib")` correctly. If a reproducer needs this pattern, document it as `lib` not the self-host directory.

- **Path A Phase 4 attempt revealed `it` keyword latent bug + A.2 regression on driver.gg 2026-05-22**. Bisect confirmed: A.1 (commit `f15a45c6`) ships clean bootstrap 2/2; A.2 (commit `2e544e84`, EFieldAccess Ptr-wrap for resource fields) regresses bootstrap.

  **Two discrete bugs identified, only one fixed**:

  1. **`it` keyword latent bug (FIXED in `bca575f8`)**. Three functions in `loader.gg` (`module_shadows_vector_iter`, `module_imports_std_iter`, `ast_mentions_std_iter_need`) used `it` as a regular variable name. But `it` is a Gorget keyword (`EIt()` in expression context, `KwIt` token). `parse_var_decl` at parser.gg:3188 uses `token_ident()` which returns `""` for keywords — so `Item it = ...` created a SVarDecl with empty name. Then `match it:` parsed as `match EIt():` (closure marker), and the lowerer's EIt→OpConstUnit fallback produced Unit-typed locals cast to Item, reading garbage tags. The Phase 3 / A.1 baseline bootstrap passed because byte layout was benign; A.2's slot allocation shift exposed it. Renamed to `item` in 3 sites — stage-1 now exits normally on `int main(): print("hi") return 0`. **Architectural follow-up**: the parser should either reject keyword-as-variable-name with a clear error OR use `non_type_keyword_name()` fallback in `parse_var_decl`. File in PARSER section below.

  2. **A.2 driver.gg-specific hang (UNDIAGNOSED, blocking E.1)**. With the `it` rename in place, simple inputs work but `driver.gg` compilation hangs in stage-1. Empirical: ~122 KB of output emitted in ~60s, then HANG with output stuck — process never terminates, gets SIGKILL'd by timeout. The hang is mid-write of `gir_liveness_diff` warnings, most likely while processing `traits.gg` functions (last warning printed: `traits___intrinsic_resolve: 17 sites`). May be related to A.2's Ptr-typed field-read locals propagating through the `traits.gg` codepath in ways that trigger an infinite loop in the lowerer itself (compile-time hang, not runtime). 

  **Suggested diagnosis path for next attempt**: instrument the lowerer with `diag_warn` calls at function entry/exit to identify which function lowering is hanging. Likely candidates: `substitute_self_placeholders`, `resolve_method_by_name`, `resolve_method_shape` (next functions after `intrinsic_resolve` per traits.gg:765+). These contain `for impl in registry.impls:` + nested loops with field-access chains — the kind of code that A.2's Ptr-wrapping affects.

- **Path A Phase 4 — REVERTED 2026-05-22 (COMMIT 3 drop emission on Phase 3 labels-only baseline)**. Implementation: `emit_scope_drops` + `emit_drops_for_early_exit` push `DropEmission` to ctx.drop_queue (replacing the no-op walks); `flush_drop_queue` stable-sorts per-block queue entries by `insert_at_inst_idx`, walks each touched block forward-pass and splices `GIDropIfAlive(local_id)` at recorded positions; flush call sites MOVED from after `wire_liveness_into_modes` to BEFORE `compute_liveness` at both `lower_function` and `lower_equip_block` (Fix B placement).

  **Empirical**: `cargo build --release` clean. `cargo test --lib --release` 1059/1061 (baseline). `cargo test --test integration --release lowerer_comparison -- --test-threads=1` 1/1 in 64.97s. stage-1 binary compiled OK; stage1.c at 597,031 lines, **1,907 `gorget_*_free(` calls** (vs Phase 3's ~0 emitted drops — drops now firing), 24 `memcmp(` drop guards, 1267 clones. **`self_host_bootstrap` 0/2 FAILED** in 1072s. SIGSEGV with same Class 8 backtrace: `str_alloc_copy ← gorget_string_clone_to_owned ← loader___type_mentions_iter ← loader___function_mentions_iter ← loader___ast_mentions_std_iter_need ← loader___should_auto_load_std_iter ← loader___load_imports ← main`. Reproduces on every input.

  **Diagnosis**: the crash is structurally a RETURN-CORRUPTION pattern. `parser___parse_source_with_offset` returns a Module containing AST items. Internal LoOwned locals in the parser body own those AST items' interior data; Phase 3 correctly labels intermediate-temps but does NOT prevent the FUNCTION-FRAME-OWNED locals from being byte-aliased by the returned Module. With Phase 4, scope-exit drops fire on those locals, freeing the AST interior. The returned Module's interior pointers dangle when `load_imports` walks it via `function_mentions_iter` → `type_mentions_iter`'s existing `gorget_string_clone_to_owned` call inside the `case TNamed(...)` arm.

  **Why Phase 3 labels alone don't help here**: the LoOwned locals inside parse_source_with_offset are CORRECTLY tagged — they DO own their data. The bug isn't in the labeling; it's in the missing CLONE-AT-RETURN-BOUNDARY semantics. When the function returns a resource-containing struct that aliases internal-LoOwned-locals' interior, the returned struct should be deep-cloned at the return boundary (or the construction should clone its inputs). Rust handles this via SSA move tracking + provenance — when the Module is built, the items vector MOVES out of the local that owned it (with proper zeroing), so the function-frame drop sees zero and suppresses. Self-host's IR construction byte-copies without proper move tracking.

  **Confirms Phase 3.5 + Rust machinery port is the only path**: Phase 4 cannot ship cleanly on Phase 3 alone. The user-selected "ship Phase 4, defer 3.5+2.3" plan is empirically falsified — the same crash signature surfaces as in all 7 prior attempts. The full architectural chain is required: Phase 3.5 (Ptr-typed dst at LIR for borrows) + Phase 2.3 (LoBorrowed→OpClone at consume) + `lower_var_decl_assign_mode` 7-branch tree at SVarDecl + `BorrowOrigin` provenance tracking + Phase 4 (drop emission).

  **Files affected (reverted)**: `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lower.gg` only (+119 / -30 lines). Working tree clean at `104cca93`.

- **Path A Phase 3.5 — REVERTED 2026-05-22 (Ptr-typed dst for LoBorrowed resource field reads, mirror of Rust `FieldLoad`)**. Attempt: in `lower.gg` EFieldAccess (~line 4284), Ptr-wrap the destination GIR type when `is_resource_type_name(field_tname, &gmod)` returns true; in `lir_lower.gg` GICallExtern (~line 2865), intercept `__field_read_*` calls whose destination slot is pointer-kind and emit `IFieldPtr + ISlotStore` instead of the byte-copying `ICallExtern`. **+138 lines total. Fast tests passed (`lowerer_comparison` 1/1 in 64s). `self_host_bootstrap` FAILED — stage-1 binary HANGS** (CPU-bound infinite loop, not deadlock; reproduces on `int main(): return 0`).

  **Root cause**: Phase 3.5 as scoped is insufficient. Ptr-typed locals propagate widely; SVarDecl's declared-type override at lower.gg:~4849 only fires when `declared_name.contains("__")`, so e.g. `String name = expr.x` becomes `Ptr(String)`-typed instead of `String`. The PARSER compiled with Phase 3.5 produces malformed AST nodes — `f.params.len()` returns garbage at stage-1 runtime, causing the loader-loop hang.

  **The depth Rust gg has that self-host lacks**: `src/ir/lowering/stmts/mod.rs:1055-1273` (`lower_var_decl_assign_mode`) is a 7-branch decision tree at SVarDecl that handles Ptr-typed sources — Branch A: declared==value, source==value (byte-copy); Branch B: declared==value, source==Ptr (clone-through); Branch C: declared==Ptr, source==value (take-address); Branch D: declared==Ptr, source==Ptr (alias); plus Branches E-G for collection-getter / view / closure-capture cases. Plus provenance tracking via `set_field_borrow` / `set_shared_heap` / `cow_register_alias` so the borrow checker knows which locals alias which aggregate fields.

  **Self-host has NONE of this consumer-side machinery.** Phase 3.5 ships the Ptr-wrap at EFieldAccess + LIR pointer-alias emission, but every downstream consumer that reads a local's type_id (SVarDecl, struct ctor args, function call args, return slots, match scrutinee bindings, ECall fallback paths, ...) needs Branch B-style clone-through-Ptr or Branch D-style alias logic. The Phase 3.5 agent estimated this as Phase 4/5/6-sized effort.

  **Files affected (reverted)**: `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lower.gg` (+23 lines at EFieldAccess), `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lir_lower.gg` (+115 lines in GICallExtern arm). Working tree clean at `f0839b6d`. Top commit remains `f0839b6d`.

  **Suggested re-framing of remaining Path A work**: defer Phase 3.5 + Phase 2.3 + Rust machinery port (`lower_var_decl_assign_mode` + provenance) as a separate multi-day architectural project. Ship Phase 4 (COMMIT 3 drop emission) on Phase 3's labels-only baseline — the byte-aliased Class 8 locals are now correctly tagged LoBorrowed so Prereq B's "skip drop registration for borrowed locals" gate them. No drops fire on byte-aliased data → no use-after-free. LoOwned locals' drops fire → original COMMIT 3 leak closure achieved. Phase 2.3's LoBorrowed→OpClone shift becomes a follow-up improvement.

- **Path A Phase 2.3 — RETRY ON PHASE 3 REVERTED 2026-05-22 (this agent)**. Phase 3 (`9f955c5c`, `add_local_inheriting` helper + 5 binding sites) shipped clean — bootstrap pair 2/2 in 1337.41s. Re-applied Phase 2.3 (the reverted `e3a45c5a` op_consume → helper delegation) on top. Fast tests passed (`lowerer_comparison` 1/1 in 63.88s). **Bootstrap crashed at the SAME `loader___type_mentions_iter` Class 8 backtrace** despite Phase 3 closing the labeled binding sites.

  **Diagnosis**: Phase 3 fixes ownership LABELS at 5 architectural binding sites (`match_scrutinee_ptr`, PBinding, PCtor field, two EFieldAccess box-derefs, ECall fallback). Labels propagate LoBorrowed/LoView semantics through the IR end-to-end. BUT the C-level byte-aliasing of struct-typed field reads is independent of labels — the LIR backend emits `__s_N = __v_M` struct-typed assignments for both LoOwned and LoBorrowed sources. Phase 3 makes the LABELS correct so Phase 2.3's helper makes a CORRECT decision (LoBorrowed at consume → OpClone), but the OpClone fires `gorget_string_clone_to_owned(&__s7)` on a byte-aliased Str — and the byte-aliased Str's `.data` is BAD memory on some control-flow path (read crashes inside `str_alloc_copy`).

  **The actual structural fix Phase 3 needs to complete Class 8 closure**:
   1. **At the GIR layer**: emit a different instruction for "borrow-bind" vs "owned-bind" at intermediate-temp sites. Currently the lowerer emits `GIAssign(dst, OpCopy(src))` regardless — at LIR/C this becomes a struct byte-copy that aliases interior pointers. The borrow-bind should emit a POINTER-typed alias (no struct byte-copy at C), OR
   2. **At the LIR backend (`lir_lower.gg`)**: when emitting an OpCopy of a struct-typed local where source is LoBorrowed, inspect the destination's ownership tag and either (a) materialize a real clone via `gorget_<Type>__clone` at the bind site, or (b) emit a pointer alias instead of a struct byte-copy. Today the LIR backend ignores ownership tags and always emits struct byte-copies.
   3. **At op_consume + Phase 2.3**: emit the clone at the CONSUME-position even when the source is byte-aliased. This was Phase 2.3's intent — but it crashes because `gorget_string_clone_to_owned(&byte_aliased_str)` reads garbage interior pointers when the alias chain produces an invalid struct (e.g. a TPrimitive Type byte-copied through __s2/__s3 then misinterpreted as TNamed by mistake).

  The clone crash at `__bb4` of `loader___type_mentions_iter` happens when the function is called with a SpannedType whose `.ty` is byte-aliased through `__s2 → __v4 → __s3`. The match dispatch reads `__s3.tag == 1` (TNamed) and enters __bb4, then reads `__s3.data.TNamed.TNamed_0` (the .name Str) and clones it. But the tag check might be wrong if the `__s2`/`__s3` byte-copies don't preserve `tag` correctly through the alias chain — OR the .data.TNamed.TNamed_0 union access reads garbage because the struct is byte-corrupted somewhere in the chain.

  **Suggested next step (option A — broader Class 8 sweep)**: audit EVERY `add_local(&ctx,` followed by `GIAssign(*, op_consume(*, *))` site in `lower.gg`, route through `add_local_inheriting`. The Phase 3 agent did 5 sites but there may be 10+. Grep candidate: `grep -A2 -B2 "add_local(&ctx," lower.gg | grep -A1 GIAssign` for sites following the pattern.

  **Suggested next step (option B — LIR backend fix)**: modify `lir_lower.gg`'s emission for `GIAssign(dst, OpCopy(src))` so when src is LoBorrowed/LoView AND src_ty is a resource-containing struct, emit a pointer alias (`__s_dst = __s_src; // borrow alias`) instead of a byte-copy. This eliminates the byte-aliasing at the LIR level, not just the GIR labels.

  **Suggested next step (option C — bind-site clone)**: at intermediate-temp binding sites where source is LoBorrowed and dst would be LoOwned (i.e., the writer expects to own), emit a real `gorget_<Type>__clone` at the bind site. This materializes a fresh-heap copy at every intermediate, eliminating byte-aliasing — but adds clone overhead at every intermediate temp.

  Phase 3's labels are CORRECT and necessary. The follow-up gap is the byte-copy at LIR/C — that's the actual aliasing source, and Phase 3 didn't touch it. Option B is the architectural reference-grade fix (mirrors Rust which keeps borrows as pointer aliases through codegen). Options A and C are partial workarounds.

Working tree clean at `9f955c5c`. Top commit remains `9f955c5c` (Phase 3 shipped).

Reset the queue after flush.

  **Crash signature is the SAME as 4th attempt** (TODO line 74 archived): SIGSEGV in `str_alloc_copy ← gorget_string_clone_to_owned ← loader___type_mentions_iter ← loader___function_mentions_iter ← loader___ast_mentions_std_iter_need ← loader___should_auto_load_std_iter ← loader___load_imports ← main`. Reproduces on EVERY input (`hello.gg`, minimal `int main(): print("hi") return 0`, and `driver.gg` itself) — the stage-1 binary crashes during `load_imports` before it can lower the input.

  **Class 8 — field-read aliasing of resource-containing struct fields** (the new survivor, predicted by the 4th attempt's "structural fix" diagnosis at TODO line 64-68). Fix C now correctly Ptr-wraps `loader___type_mentions_iter`'s parameter (`bool loader___type_mentions_iter(void* __p0)` — confirmed at stage1.c:296539). The byte-aliasing has moved INSIDE the function body, at the field-read site:

  ```c
  __v3 = ((__gg_SpannedType *)(__v0))->ty;   // byte-copy of Type (resource-containing)
  __s2 = __v3;
  __v4 = __s2;
  __s3 = __v4;
  // later, at line 397:
  __v27 = ((__gg_Type *)(&__s3))->data.TNamed.TNamed_0;
  __s7 = __v27;                              // byte-alias of caller's String.data
  __v29 = gorget_string_clone_to_owned(&__s7);  // crash if caller's heap freed
  ```

  At step 4 there are TWO byte-copies of Type (`__s2`, `__s3`); inside, the String `data.TNamed.0` aliases the caller's heap. When `load_imports`'s scope-exit drops fire (now emitted), one of them is freeing a String that `__s7` aliases, leaving a dangling pointer for the clone. This is the **SVarDecl/temp field-read class** — `auto x = obj.field` where `field` is a resource-containing struct should produce a borrow (LoBorrowed), not a fresh-owned local that's then dropped. Today the field-read assigns to a fresh `LoOwned` slot AND no clone materialises at the read site.

  **What's NOT YET addressed (Class 8)**: when `SVarDecl x = obj.field` (or the equivalent intermediate `__s2 = __v3` pattern emitted by GIR), the destination MUST be either:
    (a) LoBorrowed/BoField — propagating the source's borrow semantics; OR
    (b) LoOwned + explicit GICall to the type's `<Type>__clone` constructor — materialising a fresh-heap copy.
  Today the lowering picks neither — it emits a bare GIAssign(dst, OpCopy(src)) which byte-copies the GIR locals → at LIR/C level, a struct-typed byte-copy aliases the source's interior pointers. The drops then double-free.

  The 4th attempt's recommendation at TODO line 66-68 was "extend Prereq B beyond LoBorrowed/LoView init exprs to ALSO cover init exprs of struct types whose fields contain resources". The B-ext (commit `fe0c4b1e`) only addressed `.unwrap()` of Option/Result — not generic field-read. The Prereq B carve-out at `lower.gg:4612-4658` (SVarDecl LoBorrowed inheritance) inspects the INIT EXPR's category and routes through `inherit_borrow_from` — but only fires when the source is already tagged LoBorrowed/LoView. For a fresh `obj.field` read (which produces a `GIFieldRead` → BoField slot), the source is LoOwned/LoParam, so the carve-out doesn't fire.

  **Suggested next step**:

  1. **Most-direct fix** — at `infer_local_ownership_for_init_expr` / the SVarDecl-init categoriser, when the init expr is a field-read (`obj.field`) whose declared field type is a resource-containing struct (per `is_resource_type_name`), tag the destination LoBorrowed/BoField. The downstream emission already knows to skip drop registration for LoBorrowed (Prereq B). This closes the class without needing real clone emission — the byte-alias becomes a typed borrow.

  2. **Risk**: any sites that DEPENDED on owning a copy of the field (e.g. mutating it after reading) would now have a borrow. Audit: do any callers of `obj.field` then write to it via the local? In self-host code, this pattern is rare — typically you read a field, USE it, and discard. The few sites that mutate would need an explicit `.clone()` annotation.

  3. **Alternative — full Option 3** (per the brief's fallback): port Rust's `ensure_owned_at_consuming_arg` uniform consume-position materialization (`src/ir/lowering/stmts/assigns.rs:240-388`). This is the architecturally-correct fix that closes Class 8 AND any future field-read aliasing classes uniformly. Larger work — likely 2-3 days — but it's the keystone.

  **NEW crash class (sixth attempt)**: `free(): double free detected in tcache 2`. Backtrace via gdb-batch: `__gorget_global_dealloc_fn ← gorget_array_free ← lexer___lex_tokenize_with_offset ← parser___parse_source_with_offset ← loader___load_imports ← main`. The crash is now in the **lexer phase**, not the loader phase the 4th attempt hit — Fix C's transitive resource detection did close the SpannedType class.

  **Root cause** (different surviving aliasing class — struct constructor by-value field-init):

  In `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lexer.gg:1113` `lex_tokenize_with_offset`:
  ```gorget
  Vector[int] indent = Vector[int]()
  indent.push(0)
  Lexer l = Lexer(source, 0, indent, 0, Vector[SpannedToken](), false, false, "", base_offset)
  l.lex_all()
  return l.lex_tokens
  ```

  Lowered to stage1.c (lines 555807-555823):
  ```c
  __v18 = __s4;  // __s4 is `indent`, __v18 is OpBorrow(indent)
  __v25 = &__s13;
  memcpy(&((__gg_Lexer *)(__v25))->lex_indent_stack, &__v18, sizeof(GorgetArray));  // byte-alias!
  ...
  Lexer__lex_all(__v27);   // mutates __s14, may realloc lex_indent_stack
  ...
  __v33 = &__s4;
  gorget_array_free(__v33);   // ← my new GIDropIfAlive(indent) → DOUBLE-FREE
  ```

  The Lexer constructor inline field-init **byte-copies** the Vector struct (data ptr + cap + len + elem_size + alloc) from `indent` into `__s13.lex_indent_stack`. Both slots now point to the same heap. When `lex_all()` runs pushes that trigger a realloc of `lex_indent_stack`, `indent`'s `.data` pointer becomes stale (already freed by realloc). My new `GIDropIfAlive(indent)` then frees the stale pointer → tcache double-free.

  **Why prior attempts didn't hit this**: drops were no-op stubs, so `indent` just leaked. The 4th attempt's loader crash predated reaching this code path because their SpannedType issue crashed earlier in `load_imports`. Fix C resolved that, exposing the next class downstream.

  **The classification gap**: `classify_call_arg(&gmod, "Lexer", 2)` returns `CkCallArgBorrow` (default for unknown callees not in `fn_move_params` — struct constructors aren't registered there). `op_consume` then returns `OpBorrow(indent)` → byte-copy alias. Should be `CkCallArgOwning` so resource-typed source goes through `OpMove` (or `OpClone` if not last-use). With OpMove, `wire_liveness_into_modes` emits `GIMoveZero(indent)` after the constructor call → `GIDropIfAlive(indent)` sees zeroed slot → memcmp gate suppresses the drop.

  **Suggested next step (Fix D — struct constructor consume classification)**:

  At `lower.gg:~4527` (the ECall(EIdentifier, args) call-arg classify site) AND `lower.gg:~4520` (`is_borrow` branch's fallback), detect when `call_name` is a struct constructor (via `is_type_constructor(call_name, &gmod)` OR `gmod.type_infos.contains(call_name)`) and treat the arg as `CkCallArgOwning` rather than going through `classify_call_arg`. Tradeoffs:

  1. Pure structural fix: a struct field-init IS a consume position per CLAUDE.md "Ownership at Consuming Positions". OpClone fires when source isn't owned-and-last-use (e.g. when the source is a borrowed local). OpMove fires when liveness allows.
  2. Risk: breaks any existing call that passes a borrowed source where the struct field expects to alias (not own). Need to audit struct-literal sites for any case that depends on the alias semantics. Likely none, since byte-aliasing into struct fields IS the buggy pattern.
  3. Alternative (more conservative): register every struct constructor in `fn_move_params` with `moves[i] = true` iff the i-th field is a resource-type (per `type_infos.fields[i].type_name` + `is_resource_type_name`). This keeps `classify_call_arg` as the single source of truth and surgically marks only resource-field positions.

  **Files to touch (Fix D, alt 3)**: `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lower.gg`. Around line 7560 (the `fn_move_params` pre-scan loop), add a second pass that walks `m.items` for `IStructDef` / `IEnumDef`, looks up fields via `type_infos.contains`, and populates `fn_move_params[ctor_name]` with the per-field resource-flag vector. Then COMMIT 3's drop emission can ship cleanly — `indent` becomes OpMove(indent) at the Lexer constructor, GIMoveZero fires, drop suppressed by memcmp.

  **What was reverted**: ALL changes to `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lower.gg`. Working tree clean. Top commit remains `917be8fb`. The Fix B implementation IS correct as written — verified against the prior 4th-attempt agent's notes — but it surfaces a strictly new bug class that Fix B alone cannot close.

  **Recommendation for next agent**: ship Fix D (the struct constructor consume classification, conservative alt 3 above) as a STANDALONE commit FIRST. The triplet should still pass (no observable change because flush_drop_queue is still no-op, but `OpMove` at constructor args triggers `GIMoveZero` emission which is harmless without drops). Then re-attempt COMMIT 3 step 3 (Fix B) on top. The combined ordering — Fix A (4e4fd3f1) + Fix C (917be8fb) + Fix D (NEW) + Fix B (this agent's reverted code, ready to re-apply) — should close the COMMIT 3 attempt chain.

- **Phase 2c COMMIT 3 — fourth attempt REVERTED 2026-05-22 (agent `a731021a`)**. Brief instructed to ship step 3 of the canonical fix order: flip `emit_scope_drops` and `emit_drops_for_early_exit` from no-op stubs into queue-pushers, and turn `flush_drop_queue` into a real `GIDropIfAlive` emitter at recorded positions. Did so; **bootstrap still crashes**, but the crash mode has shifted from the original double-free in `Lexer__lex_scan_number` to a SIGSEGV in `str_alloc_copy ← gorget_string_clone_to_owned ← loader___type_mentions_iter`.

  **Key findings (new, useful for next agent)**:

  1. **Position-staleness root cause + fix** (necessary, not sufficient). First impl flushed AFTER `wire_liveness_into_modes`. That pass inserts `GIMoveZero` instructions into blocks, but the queue's `insert_at_inst_idx` was recorded BEFORE wire_liveness ran — so by flush time the recorded positions placed each drop BEFORE the `GIMoveZero` that drop_elab needs as an Uninitialized signal. Result: drop_elab classified the slot as Initialized at the drop site, kept the drop, double-freed. **Empirical**: trace showed `str_remove_char` emitting `gorget_string_free(&result)` IN THE RETURN BLOCK, immediately before `return result;` — freeing the buffer and returning a dangling Str. **Fix**: move `flush_drop_queue` to BEFORE `compute_liveness`. With drops in the block at liveness time, wire_liveness still picks OpMove at last-use (the drop doesn't count as a use, see (2)), inserts GIMoveZero AFTER the OpMove, which lands BEFORE the drop in the final layout. drop_elab then sees IMoveSlot first → marks slot Uninitialized → strips the entire guard sequence.

  2. **Self-host liveness was incorrectly counting drops as operand reads.** `liveness_inst_operand_uses` (`lower.gg:1206-1214` pre-fix) wrote `acc.set(lid, true)` for `GIDrop`, `GIDropIfAlive`, AND `GIMoveZero`. Rust's mirror (`src/ir/liveness.rs:346-348` + `:431-432`) EXPLICITLY excludes all three — drops are scope-exit side effects on the OWNED local, not reads; MoveZero is a def, not a use. With my position-fix in place AND drops flushed before liveness, the bogus read on GIDropIfAlive would extend val's live range past the OpMove site → demote OpMove to OpClone → byte-aliasing returns. **Fix**: change the three `case` arms to `pass`. This is a standalone bug-fix worth shipping even without COMMIT 3.

  3. **After fixes (1)+(2), the SReturn double-free is gone** — `str_remove_char`'s `return result` no longer frees-then-returns. Empirical: stage1.c free count 2227 → 2103 (drop_elab eliding more correctly), clone count unchanged at ~526. Stage-1 binary advances past the lexer phase that was crashing before.

  4. **NEW crash class: SIGSEGV in `loader___type_mentions_iter`**, accessing freed memory through `gorget_string_clone_to_owned(&__s7)` where `__s7 = ((Type*)&__s3)->data.TNamed.TNamed_0`. The function takes `SpannedType ty` BY VALUE, not by pointer — even though SpannedType contains Type which contains String (i.e., is a "resource type"). The self-host's `lower_function` bare-resource-param Ptr-wrapping (`lower.gg:5959-5962`, gated on `is_resource_type_name`) is **NOT firing for SpannedType** — likely because `gmod.resource_types` doesn't have SpannedType marked at the time `lower_function` runs for `type_mentions_iter`. Compare: `loader___function_mentions_iter(void* __p0)` — Ptr-wrapped (FunctionDef IS marked as resource). `loader___type_mentions_iter(__gg_SpannedType __p0)` — by-value (SpannedType is NOT marked). Asymmetry between siblings in the same compilation unit suggests **`gmod.resource_types` is populated lazily and misses transitively-resource structs** (SpannedType depends on Type, Type depends on String — only String is base-resource).

  5. **The chain that brings down the binary**: stage-0 driver compiles SOMETHING that calls `type_mentions_iter` with a Module's Type via byte-copy; type_mentions_iter walks into the by-value local, reads a TNamed payload, attempts to clone its String. The String's `.data` is a stale alias into a heap that was already freed by some upstream drop (probably in `load_imports`'s scope-exit which now DOES drop registered resource locals). **Hypothesis**: `load_imports` registers some local (e.g. the parsed Module's items vector) for drop; its scope-exit fires; this frees heap that `type_mentions_iter`'s by-value param still aliases.

  6. **The structural fix to close this class**: when `lower_function` walks parameters, the resource-type check must look TRANSITIVELY into struct fields (does this struct contain a String / Vector / Dict / Set anywhere in its field tree?). Today it consults `gmod.resource_types` which only contains DIRECT resource markers + structs the type-registration pass explicitly walked. The walk likely doesn't recurse through nested struct fields. **Suggested next step**: write a `is_transitively_resource(type_id, gmod)` helper that walks struct fields recursively; replace the `is_resource_type_name(type_c_name, &gmod)` call at `lower.gg:5961` with the recursive check. This is the keystone — without it, bare-T struct params keep aliasing and step 3 of COMMIT 3 keeps double-freeing on different code paths.

  **Empirical (this attempt)**:
  - `cargo build --release` clean.
  - `cargo test --lib --release`: 1059/1061 (baseline).
  - `cargo test --test integration --release lowerer_comparison -- --test-threads=1`: 1/1 in 63.96s.
  - `cargo test --test integration --release self_host_bootstrap -- --test-threads=1`: 0/2 FAILED in 1065s. Stage-1 binary SIGSEGV on hello.gg, on minimal `int main(): print("hello") return 0`, on driver.gg — every input. Backtrace: `str_alloc_copy ← gorget_string_clone_to_owned ← loader___type_mentions_iter ← loader___function_mentions_iter ← loader___ast_mentions_std_iter_need ← loader___should_auto_load_std_iter ← loader___load_imports ← main`.
  - stage1.c: 595,310 lines, 2,103 `gorget_*_free` calls (down from 2,227 pre-fix — drop_elab elision IS working), 24 `memcmp(` guards, 526 `gorget_*_clone` calls (~unchanged from Site 2a baseline).
  - First-attempt before flush-ordering fix: 596,028 lines, 2,227 frees, 24 memcmp, 523 clones — double-free in `Lexer__lex_scan_number` on byte-copy aliasing of `owned_slice` result. Trace showed two distinct slot pointers (`p=0xffffc5d38410` and `p=0xffffc5d39760`, ~3.4KB apart in same stack frame) freeing identical `data=0xaaaad3fd6d00 cap=3` ("1_0" buffer from owned_slice — the str_remove_char return-block bug).

  **What was reverted**: ALL changes to `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lower.gg`. Working tree clean. Top commit remains `be60ee6e`. The findings above are the deliverable — they save the next agent the day spent diagnosing position-staleness and liveness mis-classification.

  **Recommendation for next agent**: ship items (1) + (2) above as a STANDALONE commit — they are correctness fixes worth landing independently, and they unblock the more obvious half of COMMIT 3. Then attack item (6) — recursive resource-type detection — as a separate commit BEFORE re-attempting COMMIT 3 step 3. The struct-aggregate aliasing on bare-T resource params is the keystone TODO line 132-134 already flagged; today's run pinpoints SpannedType as a concrete case where it bites.

- **Phase 2c COMMIT 3 — Rust-mirror scout 2026-05-21 (agent `a594a650`)**. Brief asked to flip `add_local`'s default from `LoOwned + BoNone` to `LoBorrowed + BoNone`, mirroring Rust's CoW-default-borrow model. **Scout: delta is medium-large, NOT the actual COMMIT 3 blocker. STOPPED at Phase 1 per the brief's stop condition.**

  **Rust's actual default** (`src/ir/builder.rs:74-84` `FunctionBuilder::add_local` + `src/ir/mod.rs:450-498` `LocalOwnership::default = Untracked`): Rust defaults to `Untracked` — **not Borrowed**. New locals start with NO ownership decision; setters explicitly write a concrete state when the lowering has enough info. Drop registration in Rust (`src/ir/lowering/drops.rs:107-120` `register_local`) is **type-driven, NOT ownership-driven** — gated solely on `needs_drop(type_id, registry)`. Self-host mirrors that policy already (`is_droppable_type(type)` gate at `lower.gg:678`). The default ownership tag and drop-registration policy are **independent axes**.

  **Rust's drop-emission policy** (`src/ir/lowering/drops.rs:404-508` `emit_scope_drops_ordered`): emits `DropIfAlive(Place { local })` defensively for every registered local, **explicitly ignoring `maybe_moved`** (see `let _ = entry.maybe_moved; // kept for future invariant audits` at line 504). Snag #30 (2026-05-10 comment in the same file): "the maybe_moved tracking across nested matches + early-return paths produced a false negative... Always-conditional drop is the safe contract; the optimizer recovers the unconditional shape when flow proves it." The static-elision happens in **LIR `drop_elab`** via MoveSlot tracking.

  **The actual move-zero machinery** (`src/ir/lowering/stmts/assigns.rs:370-380` + 66 explicit `set_owned` call sites in `src/ir/lowering/`): at every consume site that transfers ownership, Rust emits `MoveZero` (or `move_zero_and_mark`) on the source's slot. This zeroes the bytes so the unconditional `DropIfAlive` at scope exit sees zeroed slots and the runtime memcmp gate (or static drop_elab elision) suppresses the drop. The LIR-side `drop_elab` then statically elides redundant guards via the MoveSlot dataflow.

  **What self-host has + lacks**:
  - HAS: drop_elab pass (`drop_elab.gg`, COMMIT 1 shipped 2026-05-21) that consumes `IMoveSlot` annotations for static elision.
  - HAS: `IMoveSlot` instruction in `lir_lower.gg:3084` emitted when it sees `GIMoveZero`.
  - LACKS: **anything that emits `GIMoveZero`**. `grep -nE "emit.*GIMoveZero|GIMoveZero\(" lower.gg` returns only handler `case` branches in walkers — no producer site. The infrastructure is wired but never fires.
  - LACKS: real OpClone emission for Ptr-typed slots (Site 2 deferred 2026-05-21; TODO line 71-75). Without this, OpClone is a byte-load no-op and multi-use of an owned local produces aliasing slots.
  - LACKS: bare-T resource param clone-on-binding (TODO line 14 + 127). Caller passes `f(struct_with_string_field)` by value, callee's struct field aliases caller's heap; no clone happens at the boundary.

  **The delta the brief proposed** (flip `add_local` default + audit `register_local_for_drop` sites + retry COMMIT 3) is mostly cosmetic: it changes the LABEL on freshly-allocated locals but doesn't add `GIMoveZero` emission, doesn't fix Site 2, doesn't add clone-on-binding for bare-T resource params. After Prereq B (commit `277e7a56`), SVarDecl's borrowed-source case already inherits `LoBorrowed/LoView` and skips drop registration — that's the structural part of the fix. The remaining gap is on the **source-of-ownership** axis (move-zero + real clone), not the default-state axis.

  **Mechanical scope of the default flip** (if pursued): `add_local(&ctx, type, NO_NAME)` is called ~100+ times in `lower.gg`. Each call is a candidate. Spot-classification: most are intermediate temps for runtime calls (gorget_array_new, gorget_string_format, etc.) where the result IS a fresh-owned value. A blind flip to `LoBorrowed` default would mis-classify ~95% of those sites; each would need explicit `add_local_with(..., LoOwned(), ...)` migration. The bare-`add_local` callers are: arithmetic temps (BOOL/I64/F64 returns), `gorget_*_*` runtime call destinations (mostly fresh-owned), struct field-read destinations (LoBorrowed/BoField is correct — already explicit at line 4025/5170 via `add_local_with`), comparison-result destinations (BOOL — primitive, irrelevant to ownership). A clean migration is a 1-2 day audit.

  **Recommended next step** — three independent commits, in this order:

  1. ~~**`selfhost(lower): emit GIMoveZero at consume sites where source is LoOwned + last_use`** — at every `op_consume(... CkAssign/CkReturn/CkCallArgOwning/CkFieldWrite)` whose return is `OpMove(lid)`, additionally `emit(&ctx, GIMoveZero(lid))` after the consume. This is the dependency of all subsequent drop-emission work. **Validate**: stage-1 should still compile clean. Drop_elab gate (post-COMMIT 1) means stage-1 perf doesn't tank — only resource-typed functions with OpMove run the full dataflow.~~ **SHIPPED 2026-05-22.** Emission landed in `wire_liveness_into_modes` rather than at every `op_consume` call site — Option B (per-inst side-channel `Vector[int] &moved_lids` threaded through `wire_one_operand` → `rewrite_inst_modes` → `rewrite_term_modes`; the walker pushes `GIMoveZero(lid)` immediately after each rewritten inst for every lid that finalized as `OpMove`). Single source of truth: only the post-pass that owns the OpMove decision can also emit the zero. See DONE.md for empirical numbers.

  2. ~~**`selfhost(lir_lower): Site 2 — emit gorget_*_clone for Ptr-typed OpClone arms`**~~ **PARTIALLY SHIPPED 2026-05-22** (Site 2a only; Site 2b still deferred). Bisection confirmed the two halves of Site 2 split cleanly along the GIR-instruction axis. **Site 2a (GICall pass-by-ptr) ships clean** — bootstrap triplet 2/2 in 1365.09s. The user-function call-arg path now emits a real `gorget_*_clone` into a fresh slot when the source slot holds a resource value. **Site 2b (GICallExtern pass-by-ptr) reproduces the historical "index out of bounds: index 0, length 0" crash in `lower___emit`** — exact same failure mode prior agents (`a474e398`, `aeebc19c`) saw. With Site 2a in place AND Step 1 (`GIMoveZero`) shipped on top, Site 2b STILL fails, so the issue isn't ownership classification — it's something in the runtime mutator path. The most-affected runtime call sites are `__field_write_*` (struct field writers) and `gorget_array_push` / `gorget_map_put` (collection mutators); these are in-place mutators where the cloned slot would need additional drop-tracking to avoid a leak-or-double-free interaction with the call's mutation. Possible avenues for the next agent:
     (a) Inspect what GICallExtern call sites carry OpClone in baseline GIR — are they REALLY the in-place mutators or do field-write paths use a different ABI? `grep -nE "(call|call_extern).*clone _" /tmp/stage1_gir_baseline.txt` should give the count; per the 2026-05-21 survey it was 169. Re-survey post-Step 1: Step 1's `GIMoveZero` emission may have flipped some OpClone sites to OpMove or vice versa.
     (b) Check whether `is_consuming_collection_method` differentiates the safe set — `gorget_array_safe_pop` / `gorget_map_remove_opt` / `gorget_set_remove` consume their source, so Site 2b's clone IS safe there. Maybe gate Site 2b emission on `is_consuming_collection_method(call_name)`?
     (c) Inspect the index-0-length-0 crash via gdb on stage-1 — what function is `lower___emit` being called from? It's by definition some lower.gg path that pushes a new instruction into `ctx.block_insts.get(ctx.current_block)`. The crash means the LowerCtx itself has empty block_insts when reading. Either (i) LowerCtx is being byte-corrupted by an aliased clone, (ii) `current_block` got set to a stale value past block_insts.len(), or (iii) the call sequence emit/new_block/switch_to got reordered by some miscompiled function.

Site 2b remains a known gap — does NOT block COMMIT 3 because the runtime mutator path it covers is byte-aliasing-but-semantically-correct for in-place mutators (per a474e398's 2026-05-21 survey: "169 callees are in-place-mutation runtimes; byte-aliasing IS the correct semantics").

With (1) + (2) shipped, the moved-out slots are zeroed AND the cloned slots have independent heap. `emit_scope_drops` records `DropEmission` entries; `flush_drop_queue` after `wire_liveness_into_modes` emits real `GIDropIfAlive` calls. Defensively unconditional — let LIR drop_elab + the runtime memcmp gate handle elision. The `maybe_moved` field becomes a future optimization, NOT a correctness gate (mirrors Rust's Snag #30 comment).
