- **Phase 2c COMMIT 3 — REVERTED 2026-05-21 (third attempt, agent `aa16d6f0`)**. The next agent's brief hypothesised that bare-T resource params (currently classified `LoParam`) were the aliasing root — `op_consume` for `LoParam` should fall through to `OpMove`, freeing caller's buffer on drop. **Hypothesis refuted on inspection**: bare resource params already get **Ptr-wrapped** at lower.gg:5879 (`is_resource_type_name(type_c_name, &gmod) → register_ptr`). Their slot type is `GtPtr`, which `op_consume` matches at line 962 returning `OpCopy(lid)` (the GtNamed-only arm at 956 doesn't fire). Then `wire_liveness_into_modes` at line 1780-1784 ALSO explicitly excludes `LoParam` from move-eligibility — `source_owns=false`, demoted to `OpClone(lid)`. So bare resource params today read as OpCopy/OpClone of pointer aliases, NEVER OpMove. Changing `LoParam → LoBorrowed` at param add_local_with sites would be a no-op for the current operand-mode decisions. The brief's Phase 2 was targeting a phantom failure path.
  **Actual root cause** (different from the brief, discovered by re-running COMMIT 3 fresh and diffing the stage-1 backtrace on driver.gg vs hello.gg):
  - On driver.gg the SIGABRT (not SIGSEGV) backtrace is **`gorget_array_free ← lexer___lex_tokenize_with_offset ← parser___parse_source_with_offset ← loader___load_imports ← main`** — `free()` itself detects the double-free.
  - On hello.gg the SIGSEGV backtrace through `loader___type_mentions_iter` matches prior agents — but on driver.gg the simpler lexer path fires first.
  - `lex_tokenize_with_offset(String source, int base_offset)` takes `source` bare and stuffs it into a Lexer struct via `Lexer(source, 0, indent, …)`. The Lexer struct holds source as a field; **Lexer__drop's recursive drop walks into the source field and frees its `.data`** — the same data the caller still owns. Classic struct-aggregate-aliasing on byte-copy.
  - This is the SAME family as the bare-resource-param diagnosis prior agents flagged (TODO line 125-128) but at the **struct-field-init** boundary, not the param-classification boundary. Per CLAUDE.md "Ownership at Consuming Positions": **struct field init is a CONSUME position; the source must own**. Today bare-T param `source` is borrow-shaped (LoParam/Ptr-aliased) AND the struct-init does not clone — both halves of the contract are wrong.
  **What the actual fix needs**:
  1. **Bare-T resource params → LoBorrowed semantics across the board** — not just rename LoParam to LoBorrowed (the brief's framing), but also surface this through `wire_one_operand` so consume sites (struct init, return, function-call ownership boundary) detect "this source is borrowed → caller still needs it → emit clone". Today `wire_one_operand` already produces OpClone for LoParam (line 1780-1788) — the right LABEL, but `lir_lower.gg::lower_operand`'s OpClone arm for Ptr-typed slots emits a no-op byte-load (COMMIT 2 Site 2 deferred). So even with correct LABELS, the clone doesn't actually MATERIALISE.
  2. **Struct-constructor calls (`EStructLiteral` / `ECall(struct_name, …)`) at field-init positions must emit OpClone when the field's declared type is a resource** — even if the source is owned (per the doc table: "Borrow, OR owned but live past this call → clone before call"). Today `lower_call` at line 4485 doesn't distinguish struct-constructor calls from regular calls; the args route through `classify_call_arg` which returns CkCallArgBorrow for bare-T callee params.
  3. **COMMIT 2 Site 2 must ship** — even with correct labels and consume-position detection, the OpClone emission has to materialise a real `gorget_*_clone(&src)` call into a fresh slot. Without Site 2, OpClone is a no-op and the byte-aliasing persists. Site 2 was deferred 2026-05-21 due to a downstream SSA / slot-numbering crash in `lower___emit` (TODO line 50).
  **The full fix is ~3 weeks of work** — bare-T param classification + struct-init clone detection + COMMIT 2 Site 2 root-cause + COMMIT 3 emission flip. Each piece interacts with the others. Shipping any single piece without the rest produces either:
  - Pieces 1-only: labels correct, behavior unchanged (no observable diff today, plumbing only).
  - Pieces 1+2: clones emitted at struct-init sites but byte-aliasing on the LIR side (Site 2 still deferred → OpClone no-op).
  - Pieces 1+2+3 without Site 2: still double-frees on the byte-aliased Ptr slots.
  - All four together: should converge to Rust-parity ~338 clones + no double-frees + no leaks (per the 2026-05-20 Path A expected-emission numbers, TODO line ~197).
  **Recommendation**: pause COMMIT 3 attempts until Site 2 root-cause is resolved. Without Site 2, the structural-rewrite at lower.gg cannot materialise clones — and without clones, drop emission double-frees on every byte-aliased resource. The Site 2 deferral was treated as orthogonal (TODO line 46-50) but it's actually the keystone: drops can't safely ship until OpClone actually clones. Filing a new high-priority task: **"COMMIT 2 Site 2 — root-cause + fix the SSA slot-numbering disturbance"**. Once that's resolved, the COMMIT 3 attempts that have been thrashing for two days will likely just work.

- **Phase 2c COMMIT 3 — REVERTED 2026-05-21 (second attempt)**. With Prereq B-extension shipped (commit `fe0c4b1e`) closing the `.unwrap()` of LoBorrowed Option/Result aliasing class, the COMMIT 3 brief expected the emission flip to ship safely. **It did not.** Same `free(): double free detected in tcache 2` backtrace as the prior attempt (`aeebc19c`): `str_alloc_copy ← gorget_string_clone_to_owned ← loader___type_mentions_iter ← loader___function_mentions_iter ← loader___ast_mentions_std_iter_need ← loader___should_auto_load_std_iter ← loader___load_imports ← main`. **What was tried** (lost from working tree but here's the recipe — implement in next attempt):

  **Empirical numbers** (Phase 3 vs Phase 2 baseline at `fe0c4b1e`), both stage-1 bodies on `driver.gg`:
  - stage1.c lines: 581,539 (baseline) → 593,421 (+11,882, +2.04%)
  - `gorget_*_free(` count: 0 → 1,998 (drops are firing — string_free + array_free + map_free + closure_free, all summed)
  - `memcmp(` gates: 23 (these are the LIR-side IDropGuardOpen pairs — 23 of the 1,998 drops go through the runtime memcmp guard; the rest are unconditional)

  **Localised failure mode**: same as prior attempt — `loader___type_mentions_iter` reads `((__gg_Type*)&__s3)->data.TNamed.TNamed_0` (a Str), then calls `gorget_string_clone_to_owned(&__s7)` — the source pointer is dangling. The function body has ZERO drops emitted; the corruption originates from a caller's scope-exit drop. **Phase-2 baseline confirms `hello_simple.gg` already segfaults on stage-1 binary** (a pre-existing self-host gap from `[bug] EIdentifier: unknown identifier 'Item'` zero-init placeholder in `loader___ast_mentions_std_iter_need`), but Phase 2 binary CAN recompile `driver.gg` successfully — Phase 3's drop emission breaks that path with the double-free.

  **Root-cause hypothesis (not yet confirmed)**: the B-extension closed the `.unwrap()` of LoBorrowed Option aliasing class, but `load_imports` registers function-scope locals (e.g., `__s7` GorgetString, `__s9` GorgetMap, `__s16/__s18` GorgetArray) for drop at the natural exit (`__bb21` in stage1.c). These were either (a) bound from `__field_read_*` of a borrowed-by-ptr parameter (not yet covered by B-extension), or (b) bound from a function-call result that is itself byte-aliased to caller-owned heap. Each candidate site needs LoBorrowed propagation or a clone-on-bind shim.

  **Specifically untouched by current Prereqs**:
  - **Bare-by-value resource params**: `void f(SpannedFunction sf)` — caller passes a byte-copy whose interior aliases caller data. The Phase 3 attempt registers the SVarDecl results from `sf.body` field reads for drop; this double-frees with the caller. Prereq B handles the simpler `auto x = obj.field` case but not the "by-value resource param → field-read of its interior" chain.
  - **`__field_read_<T>_<field>` from `Ptr(T)` param**: `void f(SpannedFunction* sf)` returns byte-copy of the field whose interior aliases caller data. Similar shape.
  - **Function-call results that return a "borrowed view"**: `SpannedFunction f = parse_function(...)` where `parse_function` returns a struct whose String/Vector fields are borrowed views into its input. Currently registered as LoOwned → double-frees with the input. Needs explicit return-place provenance tracking.

  **Path forward — three options for the next attempt**:
  1. **Universal SVarDecl borrow inheritance**: extend Prereq B beyond LoBorrowed/LoView init exprs to ALSO cover init exprs of struct types whose fields contain resources (heuristic: if init source is a function call returning a struct type, infer LoBorrowed conservatively unless the call site is explicitly a known materialisation point like `Foo()` constructor or `.clone()`). High false-positive rate; will leak some heap.
  2. **Function-result provenance tagging**: when lowering EFnCall, look at the callee's signature — if return-type is a resource-containing struct AND the call passes a borrowed param, tag result LoBorrowed/BoReturnFromBorrow. Requires signature-level analysis (which the type-checker has but lower.gg currently doesn't consult for return-value provenance). The architecturally clean fix.
  3. **Skip drop emission for SVarDecl by default; opt-in only for verifiable owners**: invert the registration policy — only register for drop when the init expression is provably an owner (literal/struct constructor with explicit clone, `!T` move from a known-owner local). Everything else stays drop-free. Sacrifices most of the leak-reduction win COMMIT 3 was supposed to deliver but ships safely.

  Option 2 is the architecturally correct path but requires touching the GIR's call-result handling and probably extending `LocalOwnership` with a new variant `LoReturnedFromBorrow`. Option 3 ships fastest. Option 1 is the wrong shape — heuristics in a place that should be type-flow-analytic.

- **Audit other method-chain sites for LoBorrowed propagation gaps** (follow-up to Prereq B-extension shipped 2026-05-21). The B-extension closes the `.unwrap()` of LoBorrowed Option/Result class — `inherit_borrow_from(ctx, dst, src)` demotes a method-chain dst back to LoBorrowed when the source was a borrow and the dst wasn't explicitly clone-materialized. Other method-chain handlers that may need the same shim once their analogous double-free shows up: `.unwrap_or(default)` (dual-arm phi merge — conservative LoBorrowed only if BOTH arms are borrows); `.borrow()` and `.iter().*` (already produce LoBorrowed via `is_collection_getter_method` for `get/safe_get/pop/last/first/remove` — verify the full surface); `.clone()` and `.to_owned()` are intentionally materialisation points, dst is LoOwned. Audit triggered by any future "double-free in method-chain" diagnosis. Files: `lower.gg:~3589-3766`.

- **Phase 2c COMMIT 1 of 4 SHIPPED 2026-05-21** (commit `94cfd4ae`): LIR `drop_elab` pass ported (~710 lines, mirrors `src/lir/drop_elab.rs:~793`). Forward dataflow / Init-Uninit-Maybe lattice / 4-phase pipeline (guard elim → memset cleanup → bool drop flags → MoveSlot sweep). Pre-gate skips functions with no IDropGuardOpen + no IMoveSlot (~99% of driver.gg functions today). Integration triplet green: 3/3 pass on `--test-threads=1` in 21:57. No observable behaviour change at this commit (drop_elab is a no-op when input has no IDropGuardOpen — lower.gg today emits none). Foundation for COMMITs 2-4 below.

- **Phase 2c COMMIT 2 of 4 SHIPPED 2026-05-21** (Site 1 only; Site 2 deferred): `lir_lower.gg::lower_operand`'s OpClone arm now emits a real `ICallExtern("gorget_*_clone", [&src])` runtime clone via `resource_clone_fn(slot_ty, &m)` when the slot holds a resource value (GorgetString / GorgetArray / GorgetMap / GorgetSet / GorgetClosure). Pointer-typed slots and non-resource types still fall through to the byte-load shape. **Site 2 deferred**: the call-arg pass-by-ptr OpClone arm was also flipped during this work but caused a stage-1 regression in `lower___emit` ("index out of bounds: index 0, length 0" panic on `gorget_array_set` — downstream propagated slot-numbering shift broke a function's LowerCtx state path). Site 2 reverted to the prior pass-through-by-address behaviour pending separate root-cause investigation. The deferral does NOT affect correctness — when the operand value is materialized via lower_operand (Site 1), the clone call fires; the Site 2 path is exercised only when a callee takes its parameter by pointer (`Ptr(T)` ABI), and in that case the callee still receives the source slot's address. **Empirical**: stage1.c `gorget_*_clone(` count 1,027 → 1,129 (+102 from Site 1). Integration triplet 3/3 green: lowerer_comparison 63.72s + self_host_bootstrap + self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point 1312.05s on `--test-threads=1`. **Next**: Site 2 root-cause investigation (likely a hidden SSA / value-types invariant that the new slot allocation disturbs) + COMMIT 3 (DropElaborator emission with `maybe_moved` filter) + COMMIT 4 (full drop emission).

  **2026-05-21 Site 2 deferral root-cause investigation (agent `a474e398`)**: Reproduced the stage-1 crash with a clean Site 2 flip following the reference-pattern shape from `emit_void_ptr_option_wrap` (`lir_lower.gg:2089-2095`). Survey result: **169 call-arg sites use OpClone in baseline GIR** (`grep -cE "(call|call_extern).*clone _" /tmp/stage1_gir_baseline.txt`). All 169 callees are in-place-mutation runtimes: `__field_write_*` (struct field writers), `gorget_array_push` / `gorget_map_put` (collection mutators), `gorget_string_format` (pure). For these callees, the byte-aliasing IS the correct semantics — the "clone" tag is just a "not last use" marker, and the runtime mutates the source in-place which is what the user wanted (same end state as a clone-then-mutate). **So Site 2 deferral is safe in practice for every current call site.**

  **Empirical confirmation of the prior agent's crash diagnosis**: with both Site 2a (GICall) and Site 2b (GICallExtern) flipped to materialise clone-into-fresh-slot, stage-0 self-bootstrap runs cleanly, produces a valid stage-1 binary (compiles + links). But stage-1 panics on its first non-trivial input (`lexer.gg`) inside `lower___emit` at `gorget_array_set(arr, 0, ...)` with `arr->len == 0`. GDB backtrace: `lower___emit` ← `lower___lower_expr` (×2) ← `lower___lower_stmt` ← `lower___lower_function` ← `lower___lower_module` ← `main`. Diffed `lower___emit`'s C output between baseline and Site-2-flipped builds: **byte-identical** (md5sum match). So the corruption originates elsewhere in stage-1's pipeline (likely SSA construction on a function whose slot-count shifted) and propagates by the time `lower___emit` reads its LowerCtx. The fix isn't in `lower___emit` itself; it's in whatever upstream pass mishandles the new slots. Time-boxed at 90 min — not enough to localise the SSA / value-numbering invariant disturbed by `lir_fn_add_slot(&f, slot_ty, "")` mid-function. **Reverted Site 2 changes; deferral status unchanged.**

  **COMMIT 3 architectural blocker (also agent `a474e398`, 2026-05-21)**: The task-as-spec'd ("flip emit_scope_drops + emit_drops_for_early_exit to emit GIDropIfAlive gated on `not entry.maybe_moved`") is **architecturally premature** — at least 3 independent gaps make it unsafe:

  1. **wire_liveness runs AFTER all drop emission**. `lower_function` calls `pop_drop_scope` (which invokes `emit_scope_drops`) at line 5732, BEFORE `wire_liveness_into_modes` at line 5751. `emit_drops_for_early_exit` is also called inline during `lower_stmt` (SReturn line 4458, SBreak line 4499, SContinue line 4504) — before wire_liveness ever runs. The only `mark_local_moved` call site is `wire_one_operand` (line 1628) inside wire_liveness. So **maybe_moved is ALWAYS false at the time emit_scope_drops runs**. The gate is a no-op. Every registered local would get its drop emitted.

  2. **OpMove emits ISlotLoad, not ISlotLoad+IMoveSlot.** Even ignoring (1), if drops are emitted unconditionally, a moved-out slot retains the byte pattern of the moved value. The LIR `IDropGuardOpen` memcmp gate (lir_lower.gg:3009) sees non-zero bytes and runs the drop — double-frees with whoever received the move. `lir_codegen.gg:3120` also emits IMoveSlot as `/* move_slot */;` no-op (mirrors Rust which relies on drop_elab static elision). So even adding IMoveSlot at OpMove sites wouldn't help without making it actually `memset(&slot, 0, size)` in codegen.

  3. **Field-aliasing.** `__field_read_X_Y(struct, idx)` returns a byte-copy of the field's value (LIR-side; no clone). A resource-typed field read creates aliasing between the struct's field slot and the destination local. Both get registered via `register_local_for_drop` (SVarDecl at line 4447). Recursive `<Type>__drop` on the struct frees the field; subsequent drop on the destination local → double-free of the same heap data. Fix requires either clone-on-field-read at the runtime layer (`__field_read_X_Y` returning an owned clone) OR aliasing-aware drop emission that detects field-read provenance.

  Other partial workarounds considered (and rejected):
  - **Move `mark_local_moved` into `op_consume`** (synchronously mark every OpMove decision before wire_liveness demotes). Avoids (1) but creates branch-divergent leaks (a local consumed only in the then-branch gets mark_moved=true → drop skipped → leak in the else-branch). Doesn't fix (2) or (3).
  - **Only register `!arg` params for drop, skip SVarDecl results.** Avoids most of (3) but still hits (2) when the param is consumed in the body. Also gives up most of the leak-reduction win (SVarDecl is the dominant register site).
  - **Run wire_liveness BEFORE drop emission.** Big refactor — emit_drops is called inline during lower_stmt; we'd need to defer all drop emission to a post-pass that walks the GIR after wire_liveness completes, or run wire_liveness on a per-statement basis. Architecturally clean but substantial work.

  **Recommendation for the next agent attempting COMMIT 3**: tackle the 3 gaps in order — (A) make IMoveSlot emit `memset(&slot, 0, sizeof(slot))` in `lir_codegen.gg::IMoveSlot` (currently `/* move_slot */;`) AND make OpMove in `lir_lower.gg::lower_operand` (line ~2371) emit `IMoveSlot(slot)` after the ISlotLoad. Drop_elab's MoveSlot sweep + Uninit dataflow then statically elides the drop guard on moved slots. (B) add clone-on-field-read for resource fields in `__field_read_X_Y` codegen — or special-case dest-local registration to skip when init expr is `__field_read_X_Y(...)` returning a resource type. ~~(C) defer drop emission to a post-pass that runs AFTER wire_liveness — accumulate scope-exit lists during normal lowering, then emit GIDropIfAlive's after wire_liveness has populated maybe_moved.~~ With (A)+(B)+(C) in place, COMMIT 3 as originally spec'd ships safely. Without any of them, it double-frees on stage-1 self-bootstrap.

  **2026-05-21 ~~Prereq (C) shipped~~** (agent `a8098340`, commit pending): deferred drop-emission queue infrastructure in place. New `DropEmission { int local_id; int target_block; int insert_at_inst_idx; int dse_scope_idx; int entry_idx }` struct + `Vector[DropEmission] drop_queue` field on `LowerCtx` + `flush_drop_queue(&ctx)` function called after `wire_liveness_into_modes` in both `lower_function` and `lower_equip_block`. Per the "simpler alternative" branch of the brief: queue + flush hook are wired, but **flush body is a no-op** — recording-side (`emit_scope_drops` / `emit_drops_for_early_exit`) hasn't been flipped to push entries yet. That flip lands in COMMIT 3. This commit ships only the structural plumbing so COMMIT 3 can plug both halves in without further LowerCtx churn. Three LowerCtx constructor sites updated (lower_function ~5748, lower_equip_block ~5953, test fn builder ~7751); each gets a `Vector[DropEmission] _de_queueN = []` and passes `!_de_queueN` to LowerCtx. Today emits no observable behaviour change: the queue is empty for every function, the flush loop is a no-op, drop-stub bodies untouched. **Tests**: `cargo build --release` clean; `cargo test --lib --release` 1059/1061 (matches baseline); `cargo test --test integration --release lowerer_comparison -- --test-threads=1` 1/1 in 64.29s; `cargo test --test integration --release self_host_bootstrap -- --test-threads=1` 2/2 in 1356.86s (self_host_bootstrap + self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point — the fixed_point pass is the strongest possible confirmation that stage1.c is byte-identical to baseline, since fixed_point asserts stage1==stage2 byte-equality after compiling the self-host through itself). **Unblocks COMMIT 3**: with the queue + flush hook in place, COMMIT 3 ships in one cleanly-scoped step — fill the recording side (3 lines in each of `emit_scope_drops` / `emit_drops_for_early_exit`) + the emission side (the `flush_drop_queue` body becomes the `block_insts.get(target_block).insert(insert_at_inst_idx, GIDropIfAlive(local_id))` loop gated on `not entry.maybe_moved` looked up via `dse_scope_idx`+`entry_idx`).

  **2026-05-21 ~~Prereq (B) shipped~~** (agent `a2f2b2b3`, commit pending): SVarDecl init's borrowed-source aliasing closed. At `lower.gg:~4442` the SVarDecl arm now reads `val_loc.ownership` (the init expression's local-ownership state, populated by `lower_expr` — LoBorrowed/BoField at EFieldAccess, LoBorrowed/BoCollectionElement at collection-getter calls, LoView/BoRuntimeView at CoW slice returns). When the source is LoBorrowed or LoView, the new `var_local` is created via `add_local_with` inheriting the same ownership + borrow_origin instead of the default LoOwned; `register_local_for_drop` is then skipped because var_local is an alias, not an owner. Owned/Param/MaybeOwned init keeps the prior behaviour (LoOwned local + register for drop). **Tests**: `cargo build --release` clean; `cargo test --lib --release` 1059/1061 (matches baseline; 2 pre-existing release should_panic failures); `cargo test --test integration --release lowerer_comparison -- --test-threads=1` 1/1 in 66.02s; `cargo test --test integration --release self_host_bootstrap -- --test-threads=1` 2/2 in 1364.39s (self_host_bootstrap + self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point). Today emits no observable behaviour change — drops still no-op (the `register_local_for_drop` skip only matters once COMMIT 3 flips emission); the change is anticipating that emission. **Unblocks Prereq A**: with var_local for borrowed-source SVarDecls now skipped from drop registration AND propagating OpBorrow at downstream consume sites, the field-read aliasing class that drove A's stage-1 segfault (`loader___type_mentions_iter` → `gorget_string_clone_to_owned` on zeroed bytes) is removed — Prereq A's IMoveSlot zeroing no longer creates a use-after-zero at field-aliased sources.   **2026-05-21 attempt at Prereq (A) — abandoned, agent `aeab2777`**: tried the brief's exact plan twice. **First try**: ISlotLoad + IMoveSlot after every OpMove in lower_operand; `memset(&slot, 0, sizeof(...))` in `IMoveSlot` codegen; left `function_needs_drop_elab` gate as-is (returns true on IMoveSlot). Result: stage-0 driver passed `lowerer_comparison` but timed out at 600s on `self_host_bootstrap` because every function with an OpMove now ran drop_elab's O(n_blocks * n_slots) forward dataflow — ~600 functions × that → minutes per function aggregation, never finished. **Second try**: gated drop_elab on `IDropGuardOpen` only (skipping IMoveSlot triggers since there's no work for them when no guards exist); restricted IMoveSlot emission to resource-typed slots only (GorgetString/Array/Map/Set/Closure + recursive_drop_structs/enums via `resource_clone_fn` predicate). Result: stage-0 driver finishes (~5 min on driver.gg), cc compiles stage1.c clean, but stage-1 binary segfaults on `tests/fixtures/hello.gg`. GDB backtrace: `str_alloc_copy` ← `gorget_string_clone_to_owned` ← `loader___type_mentions_iter` ← `loader___function_mentions_iter` ← `loader___ast_mentions_std_iter_need` ← `loader___should_auto_load_std_iter` ← `loader___load_imports` ← `main`. Translation: somewhere in `loader___type_mentions_iter`'s lowering, a GorgetString is OpMove'd (IMoveSlot zeroes the slot to {data=NULL,cap=0,len=0}) but then **a later code path reads it again and calls `gorget_string_clone_to_owned` on the zeroed bytes**, dereferencing NULL data. Reverted both A1 and A2 to keep working tree clean. **The wire_liveness pass thinks the OpMove site is last_use but it isn't.** Likely root cause: a code path through field-access or recursive function-call that wire_liveness's per-(block,inst,op_idx) liveness query doesn't model. Could be the closure-capture or the field-read aliasing case (the same family of bugs Phase 2 Prereq B was supposed to address). The interaction is: **without Prereq B's clone-on-field-read OR aliasing-aware op_consume, Prereq A's IMoveSlot zeroing creates exactly the use-after-zero the SVarDecl-from-field-read shape allows.** So (A) is NOT independent of (B) as the brief presumed — they must ship together, or Prereq A needs an additional gate that detects "this OpMove site's source is also borrowed elsewhere → don't zero". The original Snag #30 framing (agent `a15d6af6` 2026-05-20 in TODO.md line 83) prefigured exactly this: "tried both (a) emitting IMoveSlot after OpMove load + (b) making IMoveSlot emit a real memset… the combination got past link errors but still double-freed in `Lexer__lex_scan_number`". That entry was the warning; this attempt confirmed it. **Phase 2 + Phase 3 are not blocked on Phase 1 — the next agent should try them first**. Phase 2 (clone-on-field-read at SVarDecl) closes the aliasing class that probably underlies the segfault in Phase 1; Phase 3 (deferred drop emission) is structural plumbing that doesn't interact with IMoveSlot. With (B) shipped, (A)'s segfault probably disappears.

  **2026-05-21 attempt at COMMIT 3 — REVERTED, agent `aeebc19c`**: tried the brief's "flip emit_scope_drops + emit_drops_for_early_exit + flush_drop_queue" plan on top of Prereqs B + C. **Implementation shape** (revertedf):
  - Added `bool maybe_moved` field to `DropEmission` (since wire_liveness runs AFTER all scope pops, the live scope-stack is empty by flush time — `mark_local_moved` can't reach `DropEntry`s, so we mirror the flag onto the queue entry).
  - `emit_scope_drops` walks `scope.entries` LIFO at scope-pop time, recording a `DropEmission(local_id, current_block, end_of_block_pos, scope_idx, entry_idx, entry.maybe_moved)` for each.
  - `emit_drops_for_early_exit` walks innermost-out down through `target_kind`, recording `DropEmission` entries for each (excluding `exclude_local`). Position is end-of-current-block at record time (drops insert before the terminator that the caller sets next).
  - `mark_local_moved` updated to walk BOTH the live scope stack AND `ctx.drop_queue` — sets the maybe_moved flag on every matching queue entry.
  - `flush_drop_queue` rebuilds each affected block's instruction Vector (Vector has no `.insert(idx, elem)` in self-host, so rebuild is cleaner than mutate-in-place anyway). For each block, walks position k from 0..len, splices in every queued drop with `insert_at_inst_idx == k and not maybe_moved` in queue order (LIFO from the recorders → LIFO in the final block).

  **Build/lib green**: `cargo build --release` clean; `cargo test --lib --release` 1059/1061 (matches baseline); `cargo test --test integration --release lowerer_comparison -- --test-threads=1` 1/1 in 63.68s.

  **Bootstrap FAILED with double-free**: `self_host_bootstrap` + `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` both fail at runtime — `free(): double free detected in tcache 2` when stage-1 binary runs on `driver.gg`. Stage-1 binary on a *minimal* `void main(): String s = "hello"; print(s)` test case segfaults with the exact same backtrace prior agents saw:

  ```
  Program received signal SIGSEGV, Segmentation fault.
  0x0000aaaae168de2c in str_alloc_copy ()
  #1  0x0000aaaae168e150 in gorget_string_clone_to_owned ()
  #2  0x0000aaaae195733c in loader___type_mentions_iter ()
  #3  0x0000aaaae196a514 in loader___function_mentions_iter ()
  #4  0x0000aaaae196be60 in loader___ast_mentions_std_iter_need ()
  #5  0x0000aaaae196ebd8 in loader___should_auto_load_std_iter ()
  #6  0x0000aaaae169418c in main ()
  ```

  **Empirical numbers (COMMIT 3 vs baseline at `fca7303a`)**, both stage-1 bodies on `driver.gg`:
  - stage1.c lines: 581,039 (baseline) → 593,024 (+11,985, +2.06%)
  - `gorget_(string|array|map|set|closure)_free(` calls: **0 → 1,997** (+1,997 — the drop emissions are firing as designed)
    - per-type: string_free 1295, array_free 609, map_free 91, set_free 1, closure_free 1
  - `memcmp(` gates: 11 (these are the LIR-side IDropGuardOpen pairs — 11 of the 1,997 drops go through the runtime memcmp guard; the rest are unconditional drops, which suggests `lir_lower.gg`'s `GIDropIfAlive` handler isn't gating most of them)
  - bootstrap wall: 1068.90s (vs ~1364s for baseline lowerer_comparison + bootstrap triplet — so COMMIT 3 actually ran slightly faster, just crashed)

  **Localised failure mode** (in `loader___type_mentions_iter` at `/tmp/self_host_stage1.c:294784-295522`): the function takes `SpannedType __p0` by value. Body reads `__s7 = ((__gg_Type*)&__s3)->data.TNamed.TNamed_0` (a String) then calls `gorget_string_clone_to_owned(&__s7)`. The crash is INSIDE `str_alloc_copy(src=__s7.data, len=__s7.len)` — `__s7.data` is a dangling pointer. The function body itself has **ZERO drops emitted** (no `memcmp`, no `gorget_*_free` in lines 294784-295522 — confirmed via `grep -cE` returning 0). So the corruption isn't from a drop within `type_mentions_iter`; the caller (or caller's caller) ran a `<Type>__drop` recursive struct drop that walked into the SpannedType's String field and freed it, and now the by-value byte-copy held in `__p0` has a stale `.data` pointer.

  **Concrete aliasing path** (verified by reading the GG source at `loader.gg:121-132`):
  ```gorget
  bool type_mentions_iter(SpannedType ty):
      match ty.ty:
          case TNamed(name, targs):
              ...
              while i < targs.len():
                  SpannedType ta = targs.get(i).unwrap()   # <-- THIS
                  if type_mentions_iter(ta):
                      return true
                  i += 1
  ```
  `targs.get(i)` returns LoBorrowed/BoCollectionElement (Prereq B handles this case). But `.unwrap()` is lowered separately by the EMethodCall handler (`lower.gg:3665-3766`), which for resource-payload variants calls `emit_payload_read` (line 3748). `emit_payload_read` (line 5150-5177) registers the field-read dest as `LoBorrowed/BoField` (line 5168) AND emits a `<Type>__clone` if the payload type has one. **For `SpannedType` — a user struct with String + Box fields — `resolve_payload_clone_fn` returns `"SpannedType__clone"`** because `SpannedType ∈ gmod.resource_types` and `SpannedType ∈ gmod.type_infos`. But `grep -n "SpannedType__clone\|__gg_SpannedType.*clone"` in stage1.c returns ZERO matches. The clone function is **never defined** by `populate_drop_metadata` in `lir_lower.gg` for user types — only collection runtime types get clone fns. So `emit_payload_read` emits a `GICallExtern(cloned, "SpannedType__clone", ...)` whose name will NEVER resolve at C-link time… but the link succeeded. Why?

  Because the call site at stage1.c:295180 does NOT include a `SpannedType__clone` call. The unwrap path SKIPPED the clone (likely because `resource_meta_for("SpannedType")` returned None — it's a user struct, not a runtime resource). So `ta` becomes a byte-copy alias of `targs[i]`, registered as LoBorrowed/BoField. Then **Prereq B's SVarDecl gate should have inherited the borrow and SKIPPED drop registration on `ta`** — and indeed the body has no drop for `ta`. So `ta` itself isn't being double-freed.

  But the caller of `type_mentions_iter(ta)` IS `type_mentions_iter(targs.get(i).unwrap())` — `targs.get(i).unwrap()` produces an `Option[SpannedType]` whose `Some_0` payload was byte-copied from `targs[i]`. That Option is materialised on the stack (`__s40` / `__s67` at stage1.c:295474/295499) via `memcpy`. Then the body reads `Some_0` and passes it to the recursive call. **The byte-copy is fine** until somewhere a drop fires on `targs[i]` or its parent struct, invalidating the in-flight Option's payload pointers. The drop site is in **the outer `loader___function_mentions_iter` or `loader___ast_mentions_std_iter_need`** which COMMIT 3 made emit a `<Type>__drop` at scope exit on their owned `SpannedFunction` / `SpannedAST` locals — those recursive drops walk INTO the type's String/Vector fields and free them while the recursive `type_mentions_iter` is still iterating over the same data.

  **Root cause**: COMMIT 3's drops fire **at scope-exit-of-the-current-function** (the LIFO walk at `pop_drop_scope`), but the local being dropped may be a **caller-supplied by-value struct param** whose data is shared with the caller's still-live aggregate. Function entry registers `!arg` params for drop (`lower.gg:5821-5833`), but the same shape applies to bare-by-value resource-aggregate params: the callee got a byte-copy, doesn't own the contained heap, but COMMIT 3 still walks the recursive struct drop into the contained fields when it fires. Or — more directly — a SVarDecl bound to a function-call result like `SpannedFunction f = parse_function(...)` is registered LoOwned and gets dropped at scope exit. The dropping function walks into the SpannedFunction's String/Vector fields and frees them; in the meantime, a sibling/recursive call is still walking `f.body` and the String data is gone.

  **Two failure classes to address before COMMIT 3 ships safely**:

  1. **Param-aliasing for bare-by-value resource params.** The Prereq B fix only covers SVarDecl init from EFieldAccess / collection-getter calls. It does NOT cover the case where a function takes a `T` (bare resource) param and the caller passes by value — the param's contents are a byte-copy alias of the caller's data. If the param is registered for drop (currently only `!arg`, not bare), it would double-free. If the param is NOT registered (current state), but the param is used to bind a local that IS registered (`SpannedType ta = self.ty` where `ta` ends up LoOwned because `self.ty` is read via field-access), the chain of byte-copies can leave a registered LoOwned local pointing at heap the caller will free. The actual class is broader: **any heap data accessed through a chain of by-value struct copies aliases the original heap**, and drop emission must either trace the chain to skip aliased locals (impractical without type-flow analysis) or emit a clone at the binding site that converts the alias to ownership (architectural fix).

  2. **Recursive `<Type>__drop` semantics**. The auto-generated `<Type>__drop` walks ALL resource-typed fields and calls their drop. For a struct like `SpannedType { Span span; Type ty; }` where `Type` is an enum containing `TNamed(String, Vector[SpannedType])`, the drop recursively walks into String + Vector + the recursive SpannedType inside. Combined with the param-aliasing class, dropping a function's owned `SpannedType` local in the middle of a recursive walk over the SAME data (`type_mentions_iter(ta)` where `ta` aliases the parent's data) frees data the iteration is still reading.

  **Suggested next step** (a separate agent, larger scope):
  - **Phase 3 fix**: thread a "this local is an alias" provenance flag through the entire SVarDecl + EMethodCall chain. Specifically, **`.unwrap()` of an LoBorrowed Option should produce an LoBorrowed result** (not LoOwned, which is the current behaviour at `lower.gg:3940` and `5168`). The fix is small (~5 lines): inspect the Option's val_loc.ownership in the `unwrap` handler; if LoBorrowed/LoView, the unwrap result is LoBorrowed too. Similarly for `.unwrap_or(default)` — if EITHER branch is LoBorrowed, the result is LoBorrowed (conservative). And `.clone()` is always LoOwned — the clone IS the materialisation point.
  - **Phase 4 fix**: audit ALL EMethodCall lowering paths for the same provenance-propagation bug. Any method that returns an interior reference (`.first()`, `.last()`, `.get()`, `.iter().*`, `.borrow()`) should tag the dest LoBorrowed. Methods that return owned data (`.clone()`, `.to_owned()`, `.collect()`) tag LoOwned. The current `is_collection_getter_method` allowlist (line 421) is a start but only covers 6 names — the broader rule is "no method returning a byte-copy of internal state produces LoOwned".

  **Re-test signal**: with this provenance fix landed, the `type_mentions_iter` aliasing chain collapses to a series of LoBorrowed locals all the way down — none get registered for drop, and the recursive walk works on stable caller-owned data. The integration triplet should pass.

  This means **Prereqs B was incomplete**, not wrong. The fix above is "Prereq B Phase 2" — extends the LoBorrowed inheritance rule from "init expr that returns LoBorrowed" to "init expr that DERIVES from a LoBorrowed input via a non-cloning method".
  **2026-05-21 attempt at Prereq B-extension — REVERTED, agent `a3790b92`**: shipped the brief's `.unwrap()` propagation as spec'd: added `inherit_borrow_from(ctx, dst, src)` helper at `lower.gg:431` that mutates `dst`'s ownership tag in place when `src` is LoBorrowed/LoView AND `dst` is currently a borrow alias (i.e. emit_payload_read did NOT clone-materialize). Wired into the plain unwrap site (`lower.gg:3766`) after `emit_payload_read`. Helper SKIPS demotion when dst is already LoOwned (the clone case — clone genuinely produces fresh owner; demoting would skip its drop and leak). Left unwrap_or untouched: its uo_dst is LoOwned (add_local default) and the dual-arm phi merge would need conservative LoBorrowed only if BOTH arms are borrows; left as-is to keep the fix minimal.

  **Tests**: `cargo build --release` clean; `cargo test --lib --release` 1059/1061 (matches baseline); `cargo test --test integration --release lowerer_comparison -- --test-threads=1` 1/1 in 62.96s; `cargo test --test integration --release self_host_bootstrap -- --test-threads=1` shipped **1/2** (bootstrap PASS in ~10 min wall, but **`self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` FAIL** — `stage2.c != stage3.c`).

  **Empirical**: stage2.c 14,752,842 bytes; stage3.c 14,760,121 bytes (+7,279, ~0.05%). Diff is in slot ORDERING + `gir_liveness_diff` site counts ONLY — semantic operation counts are byte-identical (`gorget_array_safe_get`: 1237 in both; `memset`: 1446 in both; `gorget_*_free`: 6 in both). Stage2 has FEWER void* slots than stage3 — stage3 emits extra `&` (ISlotAddr) operations at OpClone lowering sites (`lir_lower.gg:2419`'s `blk.insts.push(ISlotAddr(addr, slot))`) for resource-typed slots whose ownership tag moved from LoOwned → LoBorrowed in the new generation.

  **Root cause of fixed-point divergence**: the B-extension fix's effect is two-generational, not one-generational. The fix changes ownership tags on `.unwrap()` results. Those tags propagate to `wire_one_operand`'s OpMove vs OpClone decision (LoOwned + last-use → OpMove; LoBorrowed → OpClone). OpClone with `slot_is_ptr_kind == false && clone_fn != ""` allocates TWO LIR slots (ISlotAddr + ICallExtern dst); OpMove allocates ONE (ISlotLoad dst). So **each ownership-tag flip is one extra void* in the lowered C**.
  - Stage1 binary was compiled by Rust gg from my-modified source. Rust gg's lowering does NOT have my Prereq B-extension fix, so when it compiled lower.gg's INTERNAL `inherit_borrow_from` body (and the SVarDecl gate, and every other site that reads `val_loc.ownership`), it allocated locals as LoOwned where the algorithm now expects LoBorrowed. Stage1 binary's COMPILED CODE thus has Rust-gg's GIR shape, not my-fix's shape. When stage1 binary runs and processes driver.gg, it executes its own (Rust-gg-shaped) bytecode + the algorithm semantics in source. The driver.gg GIR comes out correctly (the algorithm DOES inherit_borrow_from), but stage1's INTERNAL code makes Rust-gg-shaped decisions for ITS OWN operand modes.
  - Stage2 binary was compiled by stage1 binary from stage2.c. Stage2.c is the output of stage1 binary applying my fix to lower.gg. So stage2's `inherit_borrow_from`, `lower_expr`, etc. all have MY-fix-shaped GIR (LoBorrowed where stage1's was LoOwned). Stage2 binary's internal `wire_one_operand` decisions now have MORE OpClone (= more void* slots) than stage1's. When stage2 binary processes driver.gg, its OUTPUT (stage3.c) reflects this — more OpClone calls in lower.gg's own compiled code AT RUNTIME means more transient slot allocations during driver.gg's lowering, which… actually wait, this doesn't quite track because the slot allocations are for LOWER.GG's own LIR not driver.gg's.
  - Empirical convergence: stage3.c → stage4.c (re-ran stage3 binary on driver.gg) produced byte-identical C at the function-definitions level (`diff /tmp/stage3_funcs.c /tmp/stage4_funcs.c` returned 0 lines). So the system converges by stage4. The fixed-point test asserts convergence by stage3 — too strict for ownership-cascade changes.

  **Implication for COMMIT 3**: this B-extension fix is theoretically correct (per the brief) and required for COMMIT 3 safety. But shipping it alone breaks `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` for one bootstrap generation. The cleanest path forward is one of:
  - **Option A**: ship B-extension + COMMIT 3 in ONE commit, run the bootstrap test loop multiple times to converge, then verify fixed_point passes at the convergence point. Sticky if fixed_point's stage-count is fixed at 3.
  - **Option B**: relax the fixed_point test temporarily to allow N-generation convergence (e.g., assert stage3 == stage4 or stage4 == stage5 instead of stage2 == stage3). Document the relaxation, ship B-extension, then ship COMMIT 3, then tighten back.
  - **Option C**: find a way to apply B-extension's ownership-tag changes without affecting lower.gg's INTERNAL operand decisions. Probably requires bookkeeping a typed "user-code-only" flag through the lowering pipeline — invasive but fundamentally cleaner.

  Suggested for the next attempt: Option B is the pragmatic path. The fixed_point test's stage2==stage3 invariant was designed when the self-host's lowering was stable; ownership-cascade changes are a NEW class of modification that needs multi-generation convergence. Relax the assertion to "convergence by stage N for some N ≤ 4" — this is what self-host bootstraps in production compilers (Rust, OCaml, GHC) typically allow.

  **2026-05-21 attempt also confirms the brief's contingency analysis**: the brief said "The worst case without A: a branch-divergent leak ... That's a leak, not a double-free." That claim is wrong — without aliasing-aware ownership propagation through method chains, COMMIT 3 produces double-frees (not leaks). The reason: `mark_local_moved` correctly avoids dropping moved locals, but it can't help when the LOCAL is registered as owner of heap that ANOTHER local (or caller's aggregate) ALSO thinks it owns. The byte-aliasing is invisible to wire_liveness's per-(block, inst, op_idx) liveness; the redesign needs an ownership-flow that detects shared-data SVarDecls. Prereqs B is the foundation of that; Phase 2 extends it to method-chain results.

  **2026-05-21 attempt also confirms the brief's contingency analysis**: the brief said "The worst case without A: a branch-divergent leak ... That's a leak, not a double-free." That claim is wrong — without aliasing-aware ownership propagation through method chains, COMMIT 3 produces double-frees (not leaks). The reason: `mark_local_moved` correctly avoids dropping moved locals, but it can't help when the LOCAL is registered as owner of heap that ANOTHER local (or caller's aggregate) ALSO thinks it owns. The byte-aliasing is invisible to wire_liveness's per-(block, inst, op_idx) liveness; the redesign needs an ownership-flow that detects shared-data SVarDecls. Prereqs B is the foundation of that; Phase 2 extends it to method-chain results.

  **COMMIT 4** (validation/leak-measurement) is gated on COMMIT 3 shipping correctly — nothing to validate until drops actually fire.

- ~~**GIR-liveness redesign — Path A (Phases 2a+2b)**~~ **— DONE 2026-05-20** (commits `fa01b55e` (pre-cherry-pick `2dad58bb`) [Vector__T__fill plumbing precursor] + `066c836a` (pre-cherry-pick `511938a4`) [Phase 2a — wire_liveness_into_modes] + `2758696b` (pre-cherry-pick `b0b3b692`) [Phase 2b — typed ConsumeKind]). Path A — pure label-correctness foundation — is in. Phase 2c COMMIT 1 (drop_elab port) shipped 2026-05-21 (commit `94cfd4ae`); COMMITs 2-4 blocked on `CkCallArg` over-classification — see entry above. Spec preserved below.

  **What landed**:
  - Phase 2a: `wire_liveness_into_modes` post-pass replaces `tighten_owned_operand_modes`. Uses CFG-accurate `last_use_of_op` from Phase 1 to decide OpMove vs OpClone per (block, inst, op_idx). LoOwned + last-use → OpMove + `mark_local_moved`; otherwise → OpClone. Legacy `update_last_pos_*` helpers retained for `liveness_instrumentation_diff` regression net.
  - Phase 2b: typed `ConsumeKind` enum in `gir.gg` (CkAssign/CkReturn/CkCallArg/CkFieldWrite consume; CkBinOpArg/CkFormatArg/CkMatchPtr borrow); `is_consume_kind(k) -> bool` helper; `op_consume` signature gains `kind` param; non-consume kinds return OpBorrow unconditionally; 41 call sites updated with explicit kinds.
  - Precursor commit `fa01b55e` (pre-cherry-pick `2dad58bb`): `Vector__T__fill` plumbing — fixed three self-host gaps that Phase 1's cheap-win commit `5e6b3bef` introduced but didn't verify (the cheap-win's `lowerer_comparison`-only verification gate missed the LIR-link error).

  **Empirical baseline that drove the rescope** (preserved for Phase 2c work): `/tmp/stage1_probe.c` (Rust gg compiling `self_host_lowerer/driver.gg`, 550K lines) emits only **338 `gorget_*_clone(` calls + 1,607 recursive `<Type>__clone(` = ~1,945 total**, 26× lower than the naive ~51K. Rust's clone-emission is gated by THREE mechanisms in tandem: borrow-by-default at non-consume positions (~15K suppressed) + drop_elab static elision after MoveZero (~12K suppressed) + last-use detection at consumes (~2K legitimate). CoW scout (2026-05-20) + 8-site stage1.c sampling confirms: every Rust clone falls into either (a) source-is-borrowed-origin needing clone for ownership, or (b) source-is-owned-but-still-live. Zero sites where Rust cloned a clean owned local at last use.

  **Rust's actual decision tree** (grounded in CLAUDE.md "Ownership at Consuming Positions" + `docs/devbook/11-copy-on-write.md` + Rust impl):
  ```
  operand_read(local_id, position):
    if position is non-consuming:                            # bare call args, scrutinees, field reads, .get()
      return Borrow                                          # Ptr alias, zero cost
    # consuming position (push/put/set/insert/return/struct-init/closure-capture):
    match source.ownership:
      BareParam | Borrowed | CollectionElement | FieldPath:
        return Clone                                         # source doesn't own data → must clone
      Owned:
        if is_last_use_at(name, span): return Move           # source dies; drop_elab elides guard
        else:                          return Clone          # source still live; needs independent owner
  ```

  - **Path A net effect**: Stage-2.c byte-output unchanged (same operand modes at LIR-layer no-op handlers). Phase 1's instrumentation diff drops to ~zero. Foundation for Phase 2c: when emission flips, the labels are CFG-accurate so no double-frees from mislabeled loop-bodies or merge points.

  - **Phase 2c — the bundled flip (DEFERRED, ~5-7 days separate piece)**: Three entangled changes ship together because they can't ship separately without runtime regression:
    1. **Flip OpClone emission**: `lir_lower.gg:2362-2376` and `~2570-2598` — replace the `ISlotLoad` placeholders with `ICallExtern("gorget_*_clone", [&src])` when slot is a resource type. Add `clone_fn_for_type(type_id, gmod) -> String` accessor in `lir_lower.gg` parallel to `drop_fn_for_type` at `~3150`. Already-existing helper `drop_to_clone_fn` at `lir_codegen.gg:4337-4348` does the name mapping.
    2. **Port LIR `drop_elab`** from Rust (`src/lir/drop_elab.rs`, ~793 lines → estimated ~1000-1200 lines self-host). Forward dataflow on slot init-state (Initialised / Uninitialised / MaybeInitialised), worklist fixpoint on CFG. Elides redundant `IDropGuardOpen` memcmp guards at scope-exit when init state is statically provable.
    3. **Enable DropElaborator emission with `maybe_moved` filter**: flip `emit_scope_drops` (`lower.gg:~454`) and `emit_drops_for_early_exit` (`~667`) from no-op stubs to real `emit(&ctx, GIDropIfAlive(entry.local_id))` calls, gated on `not entry.maybe_moved`. Path A's `mark_local_moved` calls populate the flag → no double-frees on moved-out sites.

    Why bundled (TODO line 5 author + agent `a1c56265` both flagged): shipping (1) without (3) → orphan heap allocations (every clone leaks; today's byte-aliasing becomes independent leaking allocations; stage-2 RSS grows). Shipping (1) + (3) without (2) → double-frees return (the `xs.push("hello")` failure mode the prior DropElaborator agent hit). Shipping (3) without (1) → drops still mis-fire on aliased slots. All three together: byte-aliased pointers become independent owned clones via (1), drop_elab proves last-use slots Uninitialised via (2), DropElaborator fires drops via (3), no double-frees because no aliasing, no leak because drops fire.

  **Expected emission counts** through the phases (running on `self_host_lowerer/driver.gg`):
  - **Today (Phase 1 only)**: 0 explicit clones (OpClone is no-op ISlotLoad); byte-aliasing happens silently; drops disabled; leak at process exit
  - **After Path A (Phase 2a + 2b)**: still 0 explicit clones, but labels are CFG-accurate. Foundation only — no runtime change.
  - **After Phase 2c (bundled flip)**: ~338 clones (Rust-parity); drops fire correctly; no double-frees; no leaks

  **Stop-conditions for the Path A agent**:
  - Phase 2a: walker bug where new pass disagrees with old in a way the user can prove is "new pass wrong" (not "old pass loop-blind"). STOP, report.
  - Phase 2b: a consume-vs-borrow categorization is genuinely ambiguous at a call site (e.g. nested method calls where receiver mutating-status depends on the method). STOP, file as TODO; **DO NOT** default to consume-with-conservative-clone — that defeats the redesign.
  - Both phases: integration triplet must pass byte-clean (3/3 on `--test-threads=1`). Stage-2.c byte-output should be effectively unchanged since no emission flip.

  - Wire-in sites (already done in Phase 1): `lower_function` line ~4855; `lower_equip_block` line ~5765.
  - DropElaborator scaffolding (already in place): `DropElaborator` struct line 72, `mark_local_moved` line 564, `register_local_for_drop` line 549, `is_droppable_type` line 517.
  - **Self-host `op_consume`** (the Phase 2a/2b edit point): `lower.gg:~689-750` — currently dispatches OpCopy/OpMove/OpClone/OpBorrow solely based on `LocalOwnership`; doesn't know about position context.
  - **CoW design docs**: `CLAUDE.md` "Ownership at Consuming Positions" (declares CoW-default-borrow); `docs/devbook/11-copy-on-write.md` (full spec); `docs/devbook/13-ownership-in-ir.md` (ownership IR design); `docs/devbook/10-ownership-safety.md` (safety pass that enforces ownership rules).

- **Self-host GIDropIfAlive emission blocked on missing move-out / LIR drop_elab pair.** Surfaced 2026-05-20 by agent `a15d6af6` while implementing the scouted Phase 1+2 plan. The original assumption — "unconditional `GIDropIfAlive` is safe because LIR-side memcmp gate + runtime NULL-safe free" — proved false at runtime. Reason: the self-host's `lir_lower.gg`'s `OpMove` handler emits only `ISlotLoad`, NOT `ISlotLoad + IMoveSlot`. Moved-out slots retain the byte pattern of the moved value, so the scope-exit `GIDropIfAlive` memcmp gate (lir_lower.gg:3009) sees non-zero bytes and runs the drop on heap data that the receiving collection / return-place / `!`-arg already owns. Result: glibc `free(): double free detected in tcache 2` panic. Even with IMoveSlot wired in, the self-host's `lir_codegen.gg:3120` emits `IMoveSlot` as a `/* move_slot */;` no-op (mirroring Rust's backend) because Rust's downstream LIR `drop_elab` pass statically elides the redundant DropIfAlive — and the self-host doesn't have that pass. Tried both (a) emitting IMoveSlot after OpMove load + (b) making IMoveSlot emit a real `memset(&slot, 0, sizeof(slot))` in codegen; the combination got past link errors but still double-freed in `Lexer__lex_scan_number` on a minimal `Vector[String] xs = []; xs.push("hello")` input. Root cause: `tighten_owned_operand_modes` demotes non-last-use OpMove → OpClone, but OpClone in lir_lower.gg is currently a plain `ISlotLoad` (no refcount bump / deep copy), so two slots end up byte-aliasing the same heap pointer and both drops fire. **Phase 1 (struct + bookkeeping methods) shipped intact**; **Phase 2 wired the integration points but emission is DISABLED** with a sharp docstring + this TODO entry citing the precise gap. Subsequent Phase 3 (branch-merge `maybe_moved`) is also blocked. The scaffolding remains useful: scope tracking, local registration, snapshot/restore/union are all in place for the day a real `drop_elab` pass + actual `OpClone` semantics land. **Next steps** to unblock drop emission: (1) implement a real LIR `drop_elab` pass mirroring `src/lir/lower/drops.rs` — per-slot init-state dataflow (Initialised / Uninitialised / MaybeInitialised) that decides whether the `IDropGuardOpen` memcmp can be statically elided to a no-op or DropIfAlive can be downgraded to plain Drop. (2) Make `OpClone` in `lir_lower.gg:2362-2376` emit an actual `gorget_*_clone(&src)` ICallExtern when the slot is a resource type, instead of a plain ISlotLoad — without this, multi-use of an owned local produces aliasing slots that both try to drop the same heap data. Both fixes are multi-day work each. **Scouted file:line citations from the disabled-emission scaffolding** (still valid for the day the gap closes): LowerCtx struct now has `drop_elab: DropElaborator` field; integration sites are `lower_function` entry/exit, `lower_equip_block` entry/exit, `lower_while` / `lower_for_vector` / `lower_for_iterator` / SLoop body scopes, `lower_if` per-branch, `lower_match_stmt` per-arm, SVarDecl (register), SReturn/SBreak/SContinue (emit_drops_for_early_exit). Re-enabling drop emission once gap closes: change the no-op `_len = entries.len()` body of `emit_scope_drops` to `emit(&ctx, GIDropIfAlive(entry.local_id))` in the LIFO loop, and the matching no-op in `emit_drops_for_early_exit`. Filed alongside commits `b98e10bf` (Phase 1 — struct + bookkeeping) + `1c2ce909` (Phase 2 — scaffolding, emission disabled); pre-cherry-pick `5f281e63` + `22fc6a90` on agent `a15d6af6`'s branch.

- **OLD framing of the Phase 1+2 plan that was scouted 2026-05-20** (preserved for context; see above for the revised picture after the implementation attempt).
  The LIR-side stubs in `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lir_lower.gg:2966-3014` now correctly translate `GIDrop(local_id)` → `ICallExtern("<drop_fn>", [&slot])` and `GIDropIfAlive(local_id)` → `IDropGuardOpen(...) / ICallExtern(...) / IDropGuardClose`, with `<drop_fn>` resolved via the existing typed `drop_fn_for_type` accessor (no name matching). But `grep -rn 'GIDrop\b\|GIDropIfAlive\b' tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lower.gg` returns **zero hits** — `lower.gg` doesn't emit either instruction. The variants are dead enum tags at the GIR level; the LIR-side fix yields effectively zero leak reduction in stage-1's output C (+8 drop calls out of 8028 baseline; the +8 came from `lower_instruction`'s own newly-added locals being drop-tracked by the Rust frontend, not from any user-program GIDrop). **What's missing**: a drop-elaboration pass in `lower.gg` that walks `LowerCtx.locals`, identifies resource-typed locals via `gmod.resource_types` / `gmod.resource_metadata`, and emits `GIDrop(local_id)` at scope exit (function return, block end, break-target) for owned locals + `GIDropIfAlive(local_id)` for locals whose ownership state crosses control-flow branches. Reference: `src/ir/lowering/drops.rs` — the Rust frontend's `DropAccountant` walks the GIR builder's scope stack and emits the corresponding LIR drop guards. Self-host has the slot/local infrastructure (`LowerCtx.locals`, the scope-exit hooks in `lower_block_scoped`) but no accountant. **Why it matters**: today the self-host's stage-1 output relies entirely on (a) `<Type>__drop` recursive struct/enum drops emitted at C codegen for struct/enum field cleanup, plus (b) drops baked into `gorget_*_*` runtime functions (e.g. `gorget_array_set` clones+drops the displaced element). Function-scope owned-local drops are NOT emitted. For long-lived self-host programs (the stage-1 compiler binary itself) every resource-typed local leaks until process exit. The 2026-05-20 heaptrack "94 free calls" claim was off by ~85× (actual 8028) but the qualitative finding stands: the dominant *kind* of drop missing is per-local scope-exit drops, and those route through GIDrop. **Implementation plan — scouted 2026-05-20 by Explore agent**. Rust reference: `src/ir/lowering/drops.rs` uses `DropElaborator` (not `DropAccountant` — TODO terminology drifted from Rust). Core data structures: `scopes: Vec<DropScope>` (function/loop/block kinds) with per-scope `entries: Vec<DropEntry>` carrying `local_id`, `type_id`, `maybe_moved`. Key API: `push_scope(kind)` / `pop_scope()` (emits LIFO + topo-sort on borrow deps) / `register_local` / `mark_moved` / `emit_early_exit_drops(target, exclude)`. **Critical safety property**: Rust emits `DropIfAlive` defensively at every site; the LIR-side drop_elab statically elides the runtime memcmp when init state is provably unconditional. That contract is the foundation of Phase 1+2 viability — see below.

  **Self-host integration points** (concrete file:line citations from `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lower.gg`):
  - LowerCtx struct definition lines 28-56 — gains `DropAccountant drop_acct` field
  - `new_block`/`switch_to` lines 303-314 — block boundary primitives (existing)
  - `lower_function` entry line 4326-4327 / exit line 4378-4385 — push/pop Function scope
  - `lower_loop` line 3246 / loop-exit line 3263-3264 — push/pop Loop scope
  - `lower_if` line 3440 / merge line 3478 — push/pop Block scope per branch
  - `SReturn` line 3179-3186 — emit early-exit drops to Function scope (exclude return-place `_0`)
  - `SBreak` line 3215-3221 — emit early-exit drops to Loop scope
  - `SContinue` line 3222-3225 — same as break
  - `SVarDecl` lowering — `register_local_for_drop` after `add_local`, gated on `is_droppable_type`
  - `op_consume` line 345 — `mark_local_moved` on move-out (Phase 3 only)

  **Critical Phase 1+2 safety insight**: unconditional `GIDrop` emission on *possibly* uninit slots is **safe**, not buggy. Three things guarantee this: (a) function-entry slots are calloc-zeroed, (b) the LIR-side handler shipped in `aab784f7` already wraps every drop with `IDropGuardOpen(memcmp gate)` / `IDropGuardClose`, (c) runtime free functions (`gorget_array_free`, `gorget_string_free`, etc.) are NULL-safe per the runtime contract. Worst case for emitting `GIDrop` on an uninit local: one extra memcmp call that returns 0 and the drop is suppressed. Benign.

  This means **Phase 1+2 ships standalone, even without Phase 3's branch-merge tracking**. Phase 3 optimises the conservative drops away (avoiding the memcmp where ownership is provably unconditional); without it, Phase 1+2 is a complete correctness fix that may emit extra runtime checks.

  **Phased plan** (revised post-scout):
  1. **Phase 1: Foundation** (~1 day) — add `DropScope` / `DropEntry` / `DropAccountant` struct definitions + `push_drop_scope` / `pop_drop_scope` / `register_local_for_drop` / `emit_scope_drops` methods. No integration yet; just the data structure. Commit: `selfhost(lowerer): add DropAccountant struct + core bookkeeping`.
  2. **Phase 2: Integration** (~1.5 days) — wire into the 10 file:line points listed above. Hook `register_local_for_drop` into `SVarDecl` and param registration sites. Emit `GIDrop(local_id)` from `emit_scope_drops`. Commit: `selfhost(lowerer): emit GIDrop at scope exits (Phase 1+2)`.
  3. **Phase 3: Branch-merge** (~1.5-2 days, deferred) — add `maybe_moved` tracking, `mark_local_moved` at move-out sites, `snapshot_moved` / `restore_moved` / `union_moved` mirrors of Rust's `drops.rs:291-337`. Emit `GIDropIfAlive(local_id)` when `maybe_moved == true`. Commit: `selfhost(lowerer): add GIDropIfAlive for branch-crossing ownership (Phase 3)`.
  4. **Phase 4: Validation** (~1 day) — heaptrack on fresh `self_host_bootstrap` binary (before/after free-call count), grep validation on generated C, byte-equality across stages. Commit: `test(integration): validate drop-emission correctness`.

  **Total estimate**: 5-6 days for Phases 1-4. Phase 1+2 standalone: ~2.5 days, ships immediate correctness gains.

  **Surprises surfaced by the scout** (file these inline rather than tackle now):
  - **LowerCtx has no general scope stack** — loop_continue_stack/loop_break_stack exist (lines 40-41) but those are per-loop, not per-scope. Phase 1 adds the missing abstraction.
  - **`op_consume` (line 345) doesn't mark locals as consumed today** — self-host doesn't yet track move-out state for any purpose. Phase 3 introduces this; until then, the conservative-drop path covers correctness.
  - **`gmod.resource_metadata` is post-LIR-only** — the pass must call `build_resource_metadata(name)` directly (already established as the correct accessor for `lower.gg`-phase consumers; see the resource_meta_for-pre-population TODO entry filed during item 6).
  - **No existing late-pass architecture** — Phase 1+2 emits inline during AST traversal (matches Rust's approach), no refactoring of the lowering loop needed.

  **Risk profile**: **Low for Phase 1+2** (unconditional drops are always safe per the calloc + memcmp + NULL-safe-free chain). **Medium for Phase 3** (branch-merge ownership tracking; this is where the Rust impl had Snag #30 bugs — snapshot/restore on if/else arms). Test net additions recommended: heaptrack delta (free-call count), large-input self-host run for sustained memory check, manual grep of output C for drop-call density at function/block exits.

  Layering: the accountant lives in `lower.gg` (rule 3 — drop accounting is GIR-level, not LIR-level), reads `build_resource_metadata`, writes `GIDrop` / `GIDropIfAlive` into the GIR instruction stream. Per CLAUDE.md "Debugging heuristic — fix complexity as a signal of wrong layer": the LIR consumer fix shipped today is the necessary read-site plumbing; the actual leak fix lives upstream. [filed 2026-05-20 by agent `a90b2bbf`; scouted 2026-05-20 by Explore agent — implementation plan above with concrete file:line citations]

- **5.46 GB stage-1 RSS regression — NOT REAL on current HEAD.** Retraction 2026-05-20. The previous audit (`a825b67a`, 2026-05-19) was profiling a **stale binary** at `/tmp/memprobe/stage1_bin` left over from before the drop emission landed on `gorget-1`. Heaptrack agent `ad8bf2d8` (2026-05-20) built a fresh `stage1_bin` from current HEAD and confirmed peak RSS is **890 MB**, matching stage-0 (Rust gg debug build at ~890 MB on the same input). The "stage-1 is 6× hungrier than stage-0" gap from the original audit is closed. The audit's writer-site hypotheses (lir_codegen.gg `out = out + …` accumulators, lower.gg StructRegistry parallel storage, clone cascades) were ALL wrong — those are O(N²) CPU churn at most, not retained memory. The `sb_push` retrofit was correct CPU optimisation but unrelated to peak RSS. The follow-up 2026-05-20 GIDrop-stubs investigation also corrected the heaptrack agent's claim that stage-1 had "94 free calls vs Rust's 5,797" — the real count is 8028 (off by ~85×). Lessons: (a) **always verify the binary is fresh** before profiling — the integration test harness can leave stale artifacts that look like regressions; (b) **always verify count claims** before treating them as load-bearing — bucketing or grep patterns may be wrong; (c) **the "stage-1 is 6× faster than stage-0" framing was misleading** — most of the gap is just debug Rust vs `-O0` cc; `cargo build --release` for the host binary closes most of it; (d) heaptrack 1.4.0 on aarch64 can't process 75s/5GB traces — `heaptrack_interpret` OOMs even with the raw-mode flow. Workable substitute for future large-stage profiling: `/proc/<pid>/status` VmHWM polling + `gdb -batch -ex 'bt'` stack sampling. The agent localised the (stale-binary) 5 GB peak to `lir_codegen___compute_val_types → infer_inst_type_with_ret → find_struct_type` at line 4891 of `lir_codegen.gg` via stack sampling alone, no heaptrack. [retracted 2026-05-20, agent `ad8bf2d8`]

- **Subsidiary cleanups surfaced during the (now-retracted) audit — still worth doing**:

Either delete the comment (history lives in git) or rewrite it to describe what `sb_push` actually optimised (CPU-quadratic accumulator churn).
  - **`self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` is NOT `#[ignore]`d** despite the comment at `tests/integration.rs:13771` saying so. Verified at line 13822-13824: plain `#[test]` + `#[serial(self_host_lowerer_driver)]`. Either ignore the test until its byte-equality assertion clears, or delete the misleading comment. The test adds 3× cc invocations + 3× stage-N executions per sweep — confirm whether that's the intended cost.
  - **`build_gg_dir_cached` has only one cache slot** (`SELF_HOST_LOWERER_DRIVER`). The other 5 comparison drivers (lexer/parser/resolver/typechecker/check) rebuild 15-60s of identical work per test. Also: cache-invalidation order between test runs may have contributed to the stale-binary misdiagnosis above — worth auditing the eviction policy.
  - **Add `VmHWM` assertion to `self_host_bootstrap`.** Poll `/proc/$PID/status` for the stage-1 process, assert peak < 1500 MB (current 890 MB has headroom). Would have caught the audit's stale-binary much earlier — and catches the next real regression silently. Defer until the upstream GIDrop-emission pass lands (agent `a90b2bbf` flagged: adding it before then would either be trivially satisfied or assert against pre-existing leak behaviour unrelated to the actual fix).

## Profile snapshot 2026-05-17

Fresh `gg profile` snapshot taken on `gorget-1` HEAD (e052606e) after the post-2026-05-17 batch (validate_resource walks collapsed `4b529742`, semantic self-type index `e4e1f6a3`, GIR Liveness bitset `79499789`, propagate_copies FxHashMap `469d7942`, loader rayon `107ab8dc`, TypeTable primitive_ids `615a3d0b`, LIR cse/find_live_functions/eliminate_dead_globals FxHashMap `5c38ee14`, stack-traces v1 regression closed `01f91844`, LIR codegen perf rewrite `962ae144`). Profile output medianed over 5 runs per workload. Goal: identify the **next** dominant phase outside the known/handled zones (drop_elab, semantic name-index, safety borrow-state-reset, and the just-shipped batch).

**Workloads profiled** (release build, median-of-5):

| Workload                                            | total_ms | LIR insts | C lines |
|-----------------------------------------------------|---------:|----------:|--------:|
| `self_host_typechecker/driver.gg` (~12k aggregate)  |   152.0  |    75 262 | 193 988 |
| `self_host_lowerer/driver.gg` (~30k aggregate)      |   550.8  |   194 828 | 536 943 |

**Note on substitutions:** `target/gorget-arena/src/main.gg` and `target/gorget-js/src/main.gg` were both located but failed `gg check` at the time this profile was taken. **Arena fixed 2026-05-17** (see DONE) — root cause was a compiler bug in `register_function_signature` where an equip method `bool file_exists(self, String)` clobbered the same-named `std.fs::file_exists(cstr)` extern's `type_id` because the name-based lookup found the extern at the root scope when the equip block wasn't on the scope stack. The fix swaps to span-based lookup; arena's `gg check` is now clean. **JS still fails** with 2 `expected String, found RuntimeException` errors in `eval.gg:4266/5185` — these are real bugs in gorget-js code (snag #2 commit `547a9abd` already noted them as "Real bugs that the truncate had been hiding — gorget-js will fix them"). Filed as a separate "gorget-js: fix eval.gg host_error msg type mismatch (snag #2 surface)" follow-up below. Substituted with `self_host_resolver/driver.gg` and `self_host_parser/driver.gg` per the user's fall-back instruction. Re-run the profile against `target/gorget-arena/src/main.gg` once a fresh snapshot is desired — it should now serve as a useful third profile target alongside the lowerer and typechecker.

**Cross-workload observations:**

1. **`gir_lower` is universal-heavy** (~20-25% on every workload) — the most consistently large phase across all four. **This makes it the highest-leverage optimization target by hit rate.**
2. **`lir_optimize` scales worse-than-linearly with code size** — 32% on lowerer vs 14-19% on the others. Drop_elab dominates at the high end; the rest of `lir_optimize` is closer to flat.
3. **`load_imports` matters more on small targets** — 10% on resolver/parser, 4% on lowerer (rayon's already extracted the parallel slack on the big one). Future wins here are tail.
4. **`semantic` is consistent ~10-14%** across all workloads except lowerer (where it's diluted to 8.7%). Hot sub-passes are the same everywhere: typecheck/meta_consts/safety.
5. **`codegen` is ~15% everywhere** — flat % across sizes, confirms it scales linearly with output. The remaining 84ms on lowerer is the next-largest pure-IO phase; 56× win in `962ae144` was real but there's still tail.
