    Below this point is the original 7e plan, retained for historical reference:

    **Per-trace evidence (Explore agent, 2026-05-20)**:
    - **Vector push** (Vector__int64_t__push): erased at `src/lir/lower/mod.rs:1604-1620`. `emit_collection_constructor` at `src/backend/c_lir/helpers.rs:841-843` MUST `strip_prefix("Vector__")` to recover `int64_t`. The function signature already takes `_val_types: &[Option<LirType>]` — a hook for this exact data — but the parameter is unused (`_` prefix). Someone designed the right signature; the writer side just doesn't populate it.
    - **Dict put** (Dict__String__Foo__put): both key AND value types erased to `GorgetMap`. Consumer at `helpers.rs:875-903` parses the name with `splitn(2, "__")` to recover key/value.
    - **Vector higher-order map** (Vector__Foo__map): `map_monomorphized_to_runtime` at `src/lir/lower/calls.rs:242-251` strip-prefixes to dispatch sort/sorted/unique against the right typed runtime stub (`gorget_array_sort_int` vs `_float` vs `_str` vs `_generic`). Operand types provide zero help.

    **Existing-but-narrow precedent**: `Inst::CollectionCtor` at `src/lir/mod.rs:883-890` already carries typed `ElemMeta` (`Primitive(LirType)` or `Resource(ResourceKind)`) at *construction sites*. The parametricity exists in one corner; 7e generalises it to operand types globally.

    **The proper fix shape**:

    Extend `LirType`'s resource variants to carry element/key/value type parameters. The SSoT becomes the type itself; consumers read `operand_type.element_type()`. Mangled names become a derived C-emit-time spelling, never read for semantic decisions. Two candidate encodings to choose between in the design pass:
    - (a) `LirType::Resource { kind: ResourceKind, params: SmallVec<[LirType; 2]> }` — generalises the existing `ElemMeta` shape from constructor sites to operand types. Box[T] / Shared[T] / Weak[T] use `params=[T]`; Dict[K,V] uses `params=[K,V]`. Most rule-3-aligned but most invasive (touches every match on `LirType`).
    - (b) Augment the existing `LirType::PtrTo(StructId)` with an adjacent typed-params accessor on `StructId` itself, populated at struct registration. Less invasive but the params are now indirect (Type → StructId → params), one accessor-hop further from the operand.

    Pick (a) unless the (a) refactor blast radius proves prohibitive in spike.

    **Implementation plan** (sequenced):
    1. **Spike**: add the new variant / extend existing one. Compile-driven refactor across ~500 `match LirType` sites. Most should fall through unchanged (one new arm with `unreachable!()` or fallback). Establish that the type system compiles before propagation work begins. (~half day)
    2. **Writer-site upgrade**: `src/lir/lower/mod.rs` — preserve element types in `map_gir_type_with_structs` (line 1604-1620) and `collection_runtime_type` (line 1638). The lowering pass already parses the name once via `parse_collection_ctor_info` at `src/lir/lower/insts.rs:4050-4055`; that's where the typed info is born — don't throw it away. (~1-2 days)
    3. **Type propagation through downstream LIR passes**: `infer_inst_type` in `src/lir/types.rs:223-315`, SSA renaming in `src/lir/ssa.rs`, drop elaboration in the drop-table builder. Each pass must preserve the parametricity through value transforms. (~1 day)
    4. **Consumer migration**: now the find-and-replace. `emit_collection_constructor` reads its `val_types` parameter for real. `map_monomorphized_to_runtime`'s sort/sorted/unique arms read element type from operand. `elem_type_to_meta` simplifies (already migrated in item 7's first wave, but can drop its prefix-fallback once 7e lands). 7b becomes mechanical. Most of 7d retires. (~1 day)
    5. **Validate**: full integration sweep + spot-checks on sort/sorted/unique fixtures, dict-of-non-primitive fixtures, Box[T] / Shared[T] / Weak[T] fixtures, higher-order map/filter fixtures. (~half day)

    **Estimate**: 3-5 days. Not background-agent work — touches load-bearing IR encoding, design pass needed first, blast radius affects every backend reader. Schedule deliberately with full attention.

    **What 7e unblocks / retires**:
    - 7b becomes mechanical (10-15 lines, find-and-replace)
    - Most of 7d retires (the element-type re-parsing residue across emit_types.rs / validate.rs / lifts.rs / mod.rs falls out of the consumer migration)
    - The `emit_collection_constructor` `_val_types` parameter starts earning its keep
    - The general "name-matching for type info at LIR/backend boundary" class is **closed**, not merely reduced. Aligns with the layering-discipline doc's litmus test ("if a downstream pass reconstructs information from names … the boundary upstream was drawn wrong") — 7a-7d patched instances; 7e closes the class.

    **Why prioritise**: Gorget must be reference-grade — the right fix, not the cheap one. The current shape (parse mangled names at every consumer) is exactly the rule-2 violation the layering-discipline doc was written to retire. Every consumer that defers to name-parsing is a small ongoing tax + a future-drift risk + a stain on the codebase as a reference for how the language is supposed to look. [scheduled 2026-05-20 from Explore agent's LirType survey; user instruction: "we always want the right fix, no matter the time cost. Gorget must be reference grade, best in class, design and implementation."]

    **Blast-radius survey (2026-05-20 Explore agent)**: 215 `LirType` decision points across `src/` — 186 `match` statements + 29 `if let LirType::...`. Top hot spots: `src/backend/c_lir/mod.rs` (57), `src/backend/llvm/mod.rs` (51), `src/backend/c_lir/emit_call_extern.rs` (26), `src/backend/c_lir/helpers.rs` (18), `src/lir/types.rs` (13), `src/backend/c_lir/emit_printf.rs` (12). 52% of decision points concentrate in the C and LLVM backends — they're where the 7e payoff lands.

    **Current variant set** (declared at `src/lir/mod.rs:87-122`): `I8..I64`, `U8..U64`, `F32`, `F64`, `Bool` (scalars); `Ptr` (opaque untyped pointer); `PtrTo(StructId)` (typed pointer to registered struct); `FuncRef` (typed function ref, distinct from `Ptr` for WASM `call_indirect`); `Struct(StructId)` (address-only aggregate); `Void`. Resource types (`GorgetArray`, `GorgetString`, `GorgetMap`, `GorgetSet`, `GorgetClosure`, ref-counted families) are all `Struct(sid)` / `PtrTo(sid)` when registered. **Ptr overloading status**: `Ptr` is "untyped pointer," `PtrTo(StructId)` is "typed pointer to known struct." Both ABI-equivalent at runtime (8 bytes). No split-personality variants found.

    **Precedent for variant addition**: commit `0bd03eb8` (`feat(lir): typed LirType::FuncRef + Inst::CallByRef`) — 252 insertions across 12 files, primarily `src/lir/mod.rs` (+40), `src/backend/c_lir/mod.rs` (+81), `src/backend/llvm/mod.rs` (+88), `src/lir/ssa.rs` (+8). Variant additions are mechanical compile-driven refactors; the team has shipped this shape before. 7e follows the same playbook.

    **Encoding (a) vs (b) — open design question, NOT yet decided**: Explore agent recommended encoding (b) (`LirType::PtrTo(StructId)` + typed-params accessor on `StructRegistry`) on the grounds that encoding (a) (`LirType::Resource { kind, params: SmallVec<[LirType; 2]> }`) adds hot-path allocation across 215 match sites. **HOWEVER**: encoding (b) as the agent sketched it has an unresolved disambiguation problem — if `Vector[int64_t]` and `Vector[Foo]` both lower to the SAME `GorgetArray` StructId (which appears to be how the current registry works given there's one runtime struct per resource family), then a `StructId → params` side-map can store only ONE params tuple per StructId. The two monomorphizations would overwrite each other. For encoding (b) to work, EITHER (i) each monomorphization gets a unique StructId — multiplies registry size, but each row's params disambiguate — OR (ii) params live ON the type, not the registry (which is encoding (a)).

    Two paths forward:
    - **(a) — the conceptually clean path**: extend `LirType` with `Resource { kind: ResourceKind, params: SmallVec<[LirType; 2]> }`. The type IS the truth. SmallVec<[T; 2]> is 3 words (24 bytes); LirType grows from ~12 bytes to ~32. Pattern-matching by discriminant stays fast; only the resource arm touches params. Performance impact at the 215 sites should be measured before being treated as load-bearing. Aligns with Rust's `Vec<T>` model — the type system carries parametricity end-to-end.
    - **(b) refined**: each monomorphization gets a per-mono StructId, registry has `Vector__int64_t` and `Vector__Foo` as distinct rows pointing at the same runtime struct shape but with different params. Side-map indexed by StructId is unambiguous. But the registry's size grows with monomorphization count.

    Per the user's "Gorget must be reference-grade — the right fix, not the cheap one" stance: encoding (a) is conceptually cleaner; the perf argument should be benchmarked, not assumed. Recommendation when 7e is scheduled: spike (a) for half a day, measure compile-time impact on the lowerer driver, decide.

    **Writer-site funnel**: `src/lir/lower/mod.rs:1568 map_gir_type_with_structs` is the canonical type-mapping function. `GirType` (the input) **does not** carry collection parametricity — `Vector[int64_t]` arrives as `GirType::Named("Vector__int64_t")`, element type already erased into the name. Consequence: **7e is purely a LIR-encoding upgrade, no GIR changes required**. Parametricity threads from the name-parse at lowering → into LirType (or struct registration, per encoding choice) → out to consumers. The Explore agent identified `src/lir/lower/insts.rs:1901-1950 parse_collection_ctor_info` as the existing name-parse site for `Inst::CollectionCtor.elem_or_key: ElemMeta` — that's the precedent shape to generalise.

    **Downstream pass survival**: SSA (`src/lir/ssa.rs`) preserves slot types verbatim — no risk. `infer_inst_type` (`src/lir/types.rs:223-315`) is type-agnostic for resource-type derivation today; benefits from 7e but requires no breaking changes. Drop elaboration is slot-based, orthogonal. C backend is the primary consumer (12+ name-parsing sites retire post-7e).

    **Updated implementation plan** (5 steps; concrete file:line citations per Explore findings):
    1. **Design + scaffolding** (0.5 day): pick (a) or (b), define API. For (a): add `LirType::Resource { kind, params }` variant in `src/lir/mod.rs`. For (b): add `params: HashMap<StructId, ParamsTuple>` to `StructRegistry`, plus `set_params` / `params_for` methods. ~20 lines either way.
    2. **Writer-side threading** (0.5 day): `src/lir/lower/mod.rs:1630-1642` (where `collection_runtime_type` resolves Vector__T → GorgetArray): extract elem type from input name, write it through to the chosen storage. Also `src/lir/lower/insts.rs:1901-1950` (`parse_collection_ctor_info`): wire params from existing `ElemMeta`. ~7 lines.
    3. **Consumer migration — backend** (1.5 days): 4 sites identified — `src/backend/c_lir/helpers.rs:841-854` (Vector ctor), `src/backend/c_lir/mod.rs:314` (Dict key type), `src/backend/c_lir/emit_types.rs` (sort variants), `src/lir/lower/calls.rs:254-263` (sort/sorted/unique dispatch). ~40 lines total, mostly additive.
    4. **Validation pass** (0.5 day): new check in `src/lir/validate.rs` — for each `Struct(sid)` / `PtrTo(sid)` resource operand, assert params are populated. Ensures parametricity threads end-to-end. ~15 lines.
    5. **Retire name-parsing fallbacks** (1 day, follow-up): remove the `strip_prefix("Vector__")` legacy code from consumer sites once tests confirm the typed path is universal. ~20 lines deleted.

    **Estimate**: 3-4 days concentrated work + ~120 lines net (50 API + 40 consumer + 15 validation - 20 fallback removal). Schedule deliberately with full attention — touches load-bearing IR encoding.
  8. ~~**Fill in the remaining ~322 entries**~~ — **DONE 2026-05-19** (commit `4c35a09f`; pre-cherry-pick `fd6caf78`). 21 additional `ResourceEntry` lifted from `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lir_lower.gg::build_resource_metadata` (RESOURCES total now 31); 290 additional `RuntimeFn` lifted from `src/lir/runtime.rs::REGISTRY` (RUNTIME_FNS total now 299; matches the registry's 299 unique entries). Loader test `resources_load_clean` updated for the new counts and passes; `cargo build` + `cargo test --lib resources` + `gg check compiler/data/resources.gg` + `gg check compiler/data/schema.gg` + `cargo test --test integration httpserver_router*` all green. One drift surfaced: the spike's Dict cluster merged `GorgetDict__` with `is_typed_constructor=true`, but `lir_lower.gg` actually flags `GorgetDict__/GorgetMap__` as `false`. Left untouched per scope discipline (spike entries are frozen by item 3); added `GorgetMap__` as a separate entry with the correct flag and noted the inconsistency inline. Once items 6-7 wire consumers through `resources::table().lookup()` and surface the actual behavioral need, that spike row can be split out.

  **Estimate (remaining)**: items 6 + 7's first wave shipped 2026-05-19. Of the remaining sub-items: 7a in-flight (background agent, ~1 day). 7e is the load-bearing one — 3-5 days, design-attention work, schedule deliberately. 7b retires mechanically post-7e (~half day). 7c is gated on schema-field discussion (~half day after sign-off). 7d's bulk retires post-7e (~1-2 days residue). Total remaining: ~1-2 weeks of attention-grade work over the 7e + cleanup span.

  **Note on the original item 8 ("`gen/resources.h` integrated into C build")**: dropped from the plan. With runtime-load, there's no build-time codegen step, and the C runtime header (`c_runtime.rs`'s 20 typedef structs + 59 ABI defines) can stay hand-written — the Rust side reads sizes from the loaded resource table and validates at runtime that the values match. Adding a real generated C header is possible later if it becomes useful, but it's no longer load-bearing for the SSoT goal.

  **What this does NOT accomplish.** No language feature gain. No perf improvement (generated const data is roughly what the macro + `runtime_extern_sig` do today). No user-visible bug fix — drift class is latent, not bleeding. The win is structural insurance + alignment with the self-host-replaces-Rust trajectory: every future resource type, runtime fn, or backend is one row instead of N coordinated edits, and the shape we lock in is the shape self-host already wants.

  **Sequencing note.** Items 6-7 are not urgent — compete with truncate removal, LSP, parity work for the same weeks. Items 1-5 + 8 shipped 2026-05-19. The schema is now frozen (validated by item-5's end-to-end consumer migration); items 6-7 are bulk consumer migrations that DO suit worktree-isolated background agents (proven during item 5's sweep, agent `ad55256cd2571c832` converting 178 while→for sites was a similar shape). Plus two follow-up commits worth noting: `caa9027d fix(semantic): resolve bare-variant ctors via expected-type` (the resolver gap that surfaced during the item-5 rebase) and `770c1473 selfhost(lir_lower): import get_fn_param_types from lower` (self-host hygiene exposed in passing). [revised 2026-05-19 from audit; original entry filed earlier 2026-05-19 from `docs/devbook/18-runtime-abi.md` (runtime declaration table + future `RUNTIME_DECLS` direction)]

## Medium

- **Code smell (harness, found 2026-05-31): `c_emit_comparison` (+ the other `*_comparison` tests) label ANY non-zero `gg build --emit-c-lir` exit as `Outcome::RustCrash` (`tests/integration.rs:13627`) — conflating a genuine compiler crash with a CORRECT rejection of an error-test fixture.** Verified: all 92 "Rust crashes" are clean intended diagnostics (77 `*_error.gg` + 15 arena-escape/dup-import/control-flow error tests); 0 panics/segfaults. The label is alarming and misleading (reads as a 92-fixture Rust regression when it's the opposite — the compiler working). Fix: split the outcome into `RustRejected` (clean non-zero with a diagnostic on stderr) vs `RustCrash` (panic/SIGSEGV/timeout — grep stderr for `panic`/`signal`), and print them separately (e.g. "Rust rejected (error fixtures): 92, Rust crashes: 0"). The match-rate denominator (excl. rejected) is already correct; only the LABEL misleads. Low effort, high clarity. Apply to all 5 `*_comparison` tests that share the pattern.

- **🐛 LEAK (pre-existing, orthogonal to CoW — found 2026-05-31 during the (B) soundness apply-test): `print(struct.field)` of a String field leaks the print-temp.** `gg build --sanitize` on `struct S: String f` + `S s = S(name); print(s.f)` → LSan "Direct leak … gorget_string_copy_cow … main": the C reads the field via `gorget_string_copy_cow(&s.f)` to materialize the printf arg and never frees that owned temp. Leak count == number of `print(stringField)` calls. NOT a UAF/double-free, NOT caused by the (B) checker relaxation (baseline leaks it identically; a live-clone struct-init with NO field-print is leak-clean). Root cause: the print-arg materialize path doesn't register/free the `s.field`-read temp (likely shares root with the GorgetString materialize-net gap). Fix: free the print-arg copy temp at statement end + add fixture `print_struct_string_field_leak.gg`. Lower urgency (leak-only on a field-print idiom), but a real shipped leak.

- **c_emit parity nit (found 2026-05-31, fresh-review of the OpBorrow liveness fix): `dataframe_tier2_joins` self-host now OVER-clones Rust by ~5 (self-host ~34 call-sites vs Rust 29).** The OpBorrow-as-liveness-use fix (`8feb5c1e`) correctly moved the self-host from UNDER-cloning (~24) to clone-if-live; on this one fixture it overshoots Rust. A clone is always sound (no UAF) — this is a perf/parity nit, NOT correctness, and it does NOT move the `user_fn_count` c_emit metric (still 812). Likely an interaction with a SEPARATE pre-existing self-host CoW divergence on the dataframe join/agg path (the differential scan showed 9 fixtures changed, all borrow-after-consume CoW; the dataframe joins are the only ones overshooting). Confirm it's a distinct divergence, not a defect from the OpBorrow arm, when the dataframe c_emit cluster is next touched.

- **`Ordinal` built-in trait is never registered → wrong-signature `equip X with Ordinal:` compiles clean (latent validation gap, found 2026-05-29 during devbook ch.08 review).** The `builtin_traits` vec carries an `("Ordinal", …)` entry with the documented `int ordinal(self)` sig (`src/semantic/traits.rs:603`), but registration is gated on `scopes.lookup(name)` (`traits.rs:824`) and `Ordinal` is absent from the resolver's built-in trait reservation list (`src/semantic/resolve.rs:127`) — and no `lib/` file declares `trait Ordinal`. So `scopes.lookup("Ordinal")` returns `None` and the entry is dropped; the registry never holds an Ordinal `TraitInfo`. Consequence: `equip X with Ordinal:` (and `@derive(Ordinal)`, which desugars to one — `DERIVABLE_ENUM_TRAITS` at `derive.rs:87` includes it) finds no trait def, falls through to the inherent-impl path (`trait_def_id = None`), and its `ordinal` signature is NEVER validated. Repro: `equip Color with Ordinal: String ordinal(self): return "x"` → `gg check` reports "OK: no semantic errors"; the same wrong-signature trick on `Comparable` is correctly rejected with `MethodSignatureMismatch`. Fix: add `"Ordinal"` to the reservation list at `resolve.rs:127` (cheapest), OR declare `trait Ordinal` in a lib so it rides the user-trait path. Both the book (`docs/book/appendix-traits.md:92`) and the spec (`docs/language-reference.md:~2705`/`~2783`) document `Ordinal` as a real derivable trait with sig `int ordinal(self)`, so the implementation is out of contract with both.

- **Remove name-matching from consuming-position lowering — add an EXPLICIT per-method consuming-param signal (2026-05-25; hardened by reviewer passes 1+2).** `lower_method_call` (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/methods.rs:1850-1861`, `consuming_positions_by_name`) identifies value-consuming arg positions by method NAME (`"push"|"add"|"extend"|"send"|"push_back"|"push_front"` → pos 0; `"put"|"set"|"insert"` → pos 0,1) + an `is_string_builder_method` (`GorgetString`) exclusion. CLAUDE.md "no name matching" violation. The list feeds **FOUR** name-matched consumers (pass-2 found a 4th the title-cleanup must cover): the pre-call clone loop (`pre_call_clone_temps`, ~`:1908`), `move_zero_locals` (`consuming_positions.contains(&i)`, ~`:2143`), `consuming_clone_temps` (`consuming_positions.last()`, ~`:2235`), AND `value_arg_idx_for_method` (`:1822-1825`, same name list, drives the `expected_type` hint during arg lowering). A 2026-05-25 band-aid (commit `07649296`, `methods.rs:~1877`) added a `ParamABI::ByPtr` filter so user borrow-param methods (`SlotMap.insert`) stop being mis-treated — that closed the leak but left the name match in place.

  **Why the name list is still load-bearing**: the typed consuming signal — `fn_param_ownerships[eff][pos+1] == Ownership::Move` — is populated ONLY for Vector and Set. `register_collection_method_sigs` (`src/ir/lowering/mod.rs:3268`) hand-lists Vector (push/set/insert, `move_indices` `[1]`/`[2]`/`[2]`) + Set (`add` only) + Dict (PARTIAL: just `len`/`contains`/`clear`), and lets Deque/Channel fall through `_ => {}`. So Dict/Deque/Channel consuming methods have EMPTY ownership metadata (verified by instrumentation) and rely entirely on the name list. **Signal subtlety**: the helper `insert_collection_sig` (`mod.rs:3293-3310`) sets EVERY non-self param to `ParamABI::ByValue` regardless of consumption (even read-only `contains(key)`), so the consuming discriminator is `Ownership::Move`, NOT `ByValue`.

  **Reviewer-corrected method inventory (verify against `src/ir/lowering/builtins.rs`):**
  - `push_back`/`push_front` DO NOT EXIST anywhere — the name-list entries for them are DEAD. `Deque` uses `VECTOR.methods` (`builtins.rs:338`); its consuming methods are `push`/`insert`/`set`/`extend`/`fill`. **Deque is NOT user-constructable** (`test_deque.gg:2` "Deque does not exist in the language"; absent from `resolve.rs:~138` builtin-generic list) — so no Deque fixture is possible; cover it only via the protocol table, don't write a Deque `.gg` gate.
  - `Dict` has NO `insert`. Map consuming methods: `put` (`:352`), `set` (`:353`, alias→`gorget_map_put`), `get_or_put` (`:356`). **`update` (`:392`) is a FALSE POSITIVE — do NOT treat it as consuming** (see below).
  - `Vector.extend` (`:252`) IS in the name list but NOT registered with `move_indices`; `Vector.fill` (`:260`) is in NEITHER. Both consume the element.
  - `Set` has `add` (`:423`) AND `insert` (`:424`, alias→`gorget_set_add`); only `add` is registered.
  - `Guard.set` (`:573`), `WriteGuard.set` (`:626`), `Channel.send` (`:484`) are consuming. **But Channel/Guard/WriteGuard are opaque builtins, NOT user struct/enum templates** — they almost certainly do NOT flow through `register_collection_method_sigs` (which iterates `collector.type_instances()`, `mod.rs:3277`, filtered to `TemplateKind::Struct|Enum`, `generics/mod.rs:2230`). VERIFY; if they don't reach it, deleting the name match regresses `send`/`guard.set` → they need a SEPARATE ownership-registration path keyed off the protocol lookup for non-collection builtins.
  - `Shared.set_at` (`:516-517`, `Shared[Vector[T]]` convenience, `is_mutating:true`, `params: |a,_| vec![I64_TYPE, a.elem]` → resource value at [2]) is ALSO consuming-shaped (pass-4: its inline runtime memcpy's the element without cloning, and doesn't drop the old element — a separate latent leak). It is NOT in the current name list (the match is exact `"set"`, not `"set_at"`), so the refactor doesn't regress it. DEFER like `get_or_put`: during implementation verify whether `set_at`'s inline codegen takes ownership of the value bytes — if so add `consumes_params: &[2]`; otherwise leave empty + note. Listed here so the "scanned every protocol" claim is honest (`Shared` is `CopySemantics::Trivial`, rare with resource `T`).

  **PASS-1's recommended trigger (`is_mutating` + by-value resource param) is UNSOUND — pass-2 found confirmed false-positive LEAKS. Do NOT use `is_mutating` as the signal.**
  - `Dict.update` (`builtins.rs:392`): `is_mutating:true`, `params: key_val_params` — but the REAL arg is one whole `Dict other` (`docs/language-reference.md` update), and `gorget_map_update(dst, src)` (`emit_types.rs:~2647`) calls `gorget_map_put_cloned` — it CLONES from `other`, does NOT take ownership. Marking it Move → move-zero of `other` → leak (the exact `SlotMap.insert` failure `07649296` fixed, reintroduced). Its `params` field also LIES about the arg shape (one Dict, not key+value).
  - `Vector.sort`/`sort_by`/`sort_by_key` (`builtins.rs:~298-300`): `is_mutating:true` with an `elem_param` PLACEHOLDER, but take no arg / a comparator closure — not a consumed element.
  - Root cause: `BuiltinMethodDecl.params` is NOT a faithful arg list for `runtime_callee:None` (inline-codegen), binary-collection ops (`update`), or HOFs (`sort*`); and `is_mutating` ≠ ownership-transfer. The by-void-ptr table `collection_void_param_indices` (`backend/c_lir/helpers.rs:681`) is ALSO not a clean signal — it's a SUPERSET that includes read-only `contains`/`get`/`remove`/`index_of`/`binary_search`/`iter`/`drain` (by-ptr but non-consuming).

  **PROPER fix: add an explicit typed consuming declaration to `BuiltinMethodDecl`.** Add a field like `consumes_params: &'static [usize]` (arg positions whose ownership transfers to the callee), set per-method at the decl site in `builtins.rs` ONLY for true ownership-transfer methods: array `push`[1]/`insert`[2]/`set`[2]/`extend`[1]/`fill`[2], `gorget_map_put` key+value [1,2], set `add`[1]/`insert`[1], `heap push`[1], `channel send`[1], `guard`/`write_guard set`[1]. Leave it EMPTY for `update` (clones), `sort*` (closure), `contains`/`get`/`remove` (read-only lookups). (Runtime tables corroborate the POSITIONS — verified exact against `collection_void_param_indices`, `helpers.rs:683-704` — but that table over-includes lookups, so cross-check for position accuracy ONLY, don't copy it wholesale.)
  - **INDEXING CONVENTION (off-by-one trap, pass-3):** `consumes_params` is **self-inclusive 1-based** (self at 0, first real arg at 1) to match `move_indices` / `fn_param_ownerships[i+1]` / the runtime void-ptr table. The SIBLING `BuiltinMethodDecl.params` field is **self-EXCLUSIVE** (e.g. `push`'s `params` has elem at index 0). State this in the field doc so nobody indexes them the same way.
  - **`get_or_put` is NOT a simple lookup — leave its `consumes_params` EMPTY but for a DIFFERENT reason than stated (pass-3 correction):** on a MISS it DOES consume the default via `gorget_map_put(map, key, default)` (`src/lir/lower/insts.rs:~2929`, the `is_put` branch), AND the same `default_arg` is already `SlotStore`d into the result slot (`is_move:false`) — a genuine resource-default dual-use that is a SEPARATE LATENT ownership question (possible double-free of a resource default on miss). Do NOT try to fix that here and do NOT mark it consuming (the name list never did, so empty preserves current behavior) — but the gate MUST probe the miss-path-with-resource-default to characterize current behavior (see gates).
  - **CHURN (pass-3):** `BuiltinMethodDecl` (`builtins.rs:59-86`) has NO `#[derive(Default)]` and no constructor macro, and there are **180** `BuiltinMethodDecl {…}` literals in `&'static` tables — Rust requires every field on every literal, so adding `consumes_params` touches ALL 180 sites (≈169 of them just `&[]`). **`#[derive(Default)]` is IMPOSSIBLE** (pass-4) — `params`/`return_type` are `fn`-pointer fields (`:83,85`) and there's no `Default for fn(...)`. Contain the churn with a **declarative builder-macro** (or a hand-written `impl Default` that supplies placeholder fns, e.g. `params: no_params, return_type: ret_void`), landed FIRST; then set `consumes_params` on only the ~11 consuming entries.

  Then: **(1)** drive `register_collection_method_sigs` ownership (`Move` at the `consumes_params` positions) off this field; **(2)** derive `consuming_positions` from `ownership == Move` and delete all four name-matched sites + `is_string_builder_method` + the `ByPtr` ABI-filter (`GorgetString.push` takes an `I64` char code, `builtins.rs:677`, so it has no consuming params — naturally excluded). For Dict, source key/value `TypeId`s from `GenericCollector::instances_raw()` per-instance `Vec<Spanned<Type>>` (`generics/mod.rs:2225`), NOT mangled-name splitting (the `Dict__K__V` split-at-first-`__` at `context.rs:~555`, `methods.rs:~2067`/`~2076`, `stmts/for_loops.rs` is fragile on nested-generic keys like `Dict[Vector[int], V]`).

  **Non-collection builtins (Channel/Guard/WriteGuard) need their OWN registration path AND their own instance source (pass-3):** verified they never reach `register_collection_method_sigs` — `scan_type` (`generics/mod.rs:~390`) registers ONLY `Vector|Dict|HashMap|Set|HashSet|Box` as `TemplateKind::Struct` instances, so Channel/Guard/WriteGuard/Mutex/RWLock/Shared are absent from `collector.type_instances()`. The new ownership-registration path therefore can't iterate them from `type_instances()`; EITHER add these bases to the `scan_type:~390` allowlist (so their mangled instances are collected) OR give the new path its own mangled-instance source. Read `consumes_params` per method via `lookup_protocol(base)` / `protocol_for_mangled_name` (`builtins.rs:~989`, over `ALL_PROTOCOLS` which includes Channel/Guard/WriteGuard). Without this, deleting the name match silently regresses `send`/`guard.set`.

  **Registration mechanics:** register `fn_param_ownerships` (+`fn_param_abis`) ONLY — do NOT add Dict/Deque/Channel to `fn_sigs`. `insert_collection_sig` unconditionally writes `fn_sigs` so it CAN'T be reused — write a new ownership-only path. The reason is NOT the runtime redirect (`map_monomorphized_to_runtime`, `lir/lower/calls.rs:~270`, keys off the mangled name, never `fn_sigs` — Vector/Set ARE in fn_sigs and redirect fine). It's **return-type-resolution precedence**: `methods.rs:~2051` prefers `fn_sig_ret` (`:~1968`) over `resolve_builtin_method_return_type` (`:~2109`); a sig with a wrong/opaque-Dict return type would change that path. Ownership-only registration is layering-COHERENT and COMPLEMENTARY to already-shipped work: `populate_consume_externs` (`mod.rs:1860`, comment names `Dict__K__V__put` at `:1856`) already derives `Module::consume_externs` (shipped, see DONE.md) from ANY `fn_param_ownerships` `Move` param, independent of fn_sigs — this fix populates more Move entries and consume_externs picks them up automatically (no conflict).

  **⚠️ Blast radius (pass-2):** `consume_externs` feeds the FATAL Tier-2a consume-site validator (`mod.rs:~1870`). A false-positive `Move` (e.g. on `update`/`sort*`) becomes a COMPILE error, not merely a leak — so getting `consumes_params` wrong fails loud, but test for it.

  **Validation gates (the obvious ones are BLIND to the at-risk methods, and existing leak fixtures use only PRIMITIVE int values):** `cargo test --lib`; full integration; `slotmap_cycle_no_leak` + leak probes (`mem_live()`-delta `leaked={delta>0}` / LSan alloc-balance — sound). MUST add resource-`T` (not int) leak-probe fixtures for: Dict `put`/`set`, **`Channel[String].send`** (pass-2: omitted from the prior gate list; highest-risk given the non-collection-registration question), `Guard`/`WriteGuard` `[String].set`, Vector `extend`/`fill`, Set `insert`. AND a **`Dict.update(other_with_resource_values)` probe that asserts NO leak (NON-consuming)** — a fixture written to pass-1's framing would assert the wrong (leaky) behavior. AND a **`Dict.get_or_put` MISS-path probe with a resource-typed default** (pass-3) — the default is both `gorget_map_put`'d into the map and SlotStored into the result on miss; this probe characterizes that dual-use (do NOT "fix" it in this refactor, just gate that current behavior doesn't regress). Deque: protocol-table only (not constructable). Revert if it regresses (the consuming-clone leak fix is separate commit `07649296`, safe to keep).

  **Current state is CORRECT** — the name list is ABI-filtered (`07649296`), so this is a cleanliness/architecture refactor (and it closes the pre-existing `get_or_put`/`fill`/`extend` gaps), NOT a bug fix. There is no live bug forcing it; shipping the `is_mutating` trigger would trade a clean-but-name-matched correct state for a clean-looking-but-LEAKY one (`update`/`sort*`). Only proceed with the explicit `consumes_params` signal.

- **Drop the imported-module typecheck-error truncate (writer-site fixes).** `check_items_recursive_tc` in `src/semantic/typecheck.rs` truncates ALL errors from `Item::Module` (imported modules) to silence false positives from a "foreign scope context". Snag #2 (2026-05-17) added a `hard_errors` sidecar so concrete-vs-concrete call-arg mismatches survive — but the blanket truncate still hides everything else.

  **STATUS 2026-05-19 — fix (a) shipped; truncate still in place.** The byte-literal class (a) is closed at the writer site: BinaryOp now threads `decl_type_hint` from a fully-concrete integer-primitive LHS into a bare integer-literal RHS, so `byte c == b'X'` types as `uint8 == uint8` without losing the LHS context. Call-arg / VarDecl positions already worked via existing `decl_type_hint` plumbing; the BinaryOp arm was the gap. Same writer-site idiom that Unary already uses for `-IntLiteral`. Fixtures: `tests/fixtures/self_host_lexer/lexer.gg`'s 130+ byte-literal sites now type-check clean.

  **What's left blocking the truncate removal.** Diagnostic run with truncate temporarily disabled (and my (a) fix in place) surfaced these residual errors across the six self-host drivers — none of them match the original (b)/(c) classes described in the brief:
  - **Stale self-host code missing newer enum variants** — `EFString`, `ETry`, `EMove`, `EMutableBorrow`, `EAs`, `EAwait`, `ESpawn`, `ESpawnBlocking`, `ERethrow`, `ECatch` (Expr variants); `SMeta`, `SAssertReturn`, `SSnapshot`, `SMetaForMatch`, `SWith`, `SAssert`, `SNamedScope`, `SSelect`, `SOnError`, `SItem` (Stmt variants); `KwCatch`, `KwOn`, `KwSnapshot` (keyword variants); `GTNone` (GirType variants) — at non-exhaustive `match` sites in `self_host_{parser,resolver,typechecker,lowerer,check}/{parser,resolve,format_gir,lower}.gg`. These are real errors; self-host hasn't kept up with newer AST variants. Fixing requires updating all five driver directories to add catch-alls or proper handling.
  - **Real bug**: `no field 'span' found on type 'SpannedToken'` in `self_host_{parser,resolver}/parser.gg:2512`/`2485` (the `snap_name_tok.span` access).
  - **(b) and (c) NOT OBSERVED** — the self-host parser.gg has no `throws` functions, so the auto-prop unbound-Var-Ok class claimed in the brief doesn't manifest here. `eval.gg:1528`-style match-arm result mismatches are in gorget-js code (separate repo), not in our tree. Either the brief was based on a different snapshot, or those classes need to be re-surveyed against current code before fixing.

  **Next step (deferred):** retire the stale self-host code (add the missing match arms / catch-alls) across all five driver directories, fix the SpannedToken.span access, THEN try removing the truncate. Estimate: ~1-2 days for the self-host modernization sweep, plus a focused retry on (b)/(c) once we have a concrete fixture surfacing them.

  **Why not bull through and remove the truncate now**: the surfacing errors are real bugs (stale code), not the "foreign-scope false positives" the truncate's docstring claims. Removing the truncate would make `gg check` fail on the self-host drivers — but the fix isn't a compiler change, it's a self-host code update. That's a separate body of work from the writer-site brief. Per CLAUDE.md "Don't redesign around compiler gaps" — these aren't compiler gaps, so the rule cuts the other way: fix the self-host code, don't leave the truncate as a perma-mask.

  Once everything else is clean, the entire truncate (`errors.truncate(error_count)` block at typecheck.rs ~6071) plus the `hard_errors` field + `hard_type_mismatch` call sites at ~1364-1394 can be deleted; the signal becomes redundant when concrete-vs-concrete arg mismatches are normal `errors` again. [revised: 2026-05-19, fix (a) shipped via BinaryOp decl_type_hint thread]

- **`--clones=stats` per-CloneId runtime breakdown.** Today `--clones=stats` ships exactly the historical `--clone-stats` aggregate line (`array_clone=N map_clone=N …`). The compile-time half landed with `CloneId` as the join-key axis: every `ImplicitCloneWarning` carries an `id: CloneId`, and `--clones=verbose` renders `(id, span, type, reason, size_bytes, runtime_fn)` per site. The remaining work is the runtime side: thread `CloneId` from `ImplicitCloneWarning` through LIR lowering down to each runtime clone-call emission site (`gorget_array_clone`, `gorget_map_clone`, `<UserStruct>__clone`, …), allocate a per-id `_Atomic size_t` counter array sized at module finalization (`N = next_clone_id`), and bump `__gorget_clone_count[id]` at each call. The atexit handler then emits a per-id breakdown after the aggregate line when `--clones=verbose,stats` (or `all`). Design sketch: (a) record `clone_id_at_call: FxHashMap<InstId, CloneId>` on `LirModule` populated by the clone-emit sites in `ensure_owned_at_boundary` / `clone_fn_for_ptr` callers; (b) c_lir's `emit_extern_call` consults the map and pre-pends `__gorget_clone_count[<id>]++;` to the call expression; (c) c_runtime emits `static _Atomic size_t __gorget_clone_count[N] = {0};` from `LirModule.next_clone_id`. Blocker today: each warning is emitted at `warn_implicit_clone` time but the corresponding runtime call is materialized later (LIR lowering); the link between (warning id) and (LIR instruction) is not currently captured. The cleanest fix is to make `warn_implicit_clone` return the new `CloneId`, then thread it through `ensure_owned_at_boundary` to the LIR Inst::CallExtern as a typed sidecar — substantial plumbing but mechanical. Estimate: 1-2 days. [added: 2026-05-17, follow-up to --clones unification]

- **LSP server.** `language-design.md` lists LSP as a design target but nothing ships today. The gorget-js agent flagged this as the single biggest developer-experience gap: estimated 50% of their loops were "what's that field again?" / "how is this method spelled?" — for a language with rich types, no autocomplete/hover/go-to-def is a heavy accessibility loss. Biggest payoff, biggest investment among the gorget-js critique items — don't start until the smaller papercuts (lint:suggest_throws, import aliasing, parser fixes, stack traces, --clones=verbose) are addressed; those are days each, LSP is multi-week. The semantic analyzer already builds a complete typed scope tree (`ScopeTable` + `function_info` + `TypeRegistry`) — the foundation is there; the work is the LSP-protocol layer, file-watching for incremental updates, and the inevitable "what does Gorget's hover-on-`x.method` look like in practice" design pass. [added: 2026-05-12, from gorget-js critique]

- **`panic` as builtin — option (a) follow-on: retire the hardcoded `gorget_panic` lowering at `assert`.** Option (b) shipped 2026-05-13: `panic(msg)` is callable from user code, typechecks as Never (compatible with any expected type), and registers `gorget_panic` in `noreturn_fns` for indirect call paths. The hardcoded `call_extern("gorget_panic", …)` at `src/ir/lowering/stmts/mod.rs:2132` for `assert` lowering remains. Option (a) (layering-discipline-correct answer) would: declare `panic` in a stdlib module as `extern noreturn void panic(String msg)`, route the `assert` failure path through a normal `panic(msg)` call, retire the name-match. Defer until the prelude / auto-import machinery is fit for purpose (today only enum variants prelude-import — `panic` needs to be globally available without `from std.X import panic`). Also audit `lib/freestanding/runtime.c` and `c_runtime.rs` for any other `_Noreturn` C functions exposed to Gorget (likely none today). [revised 2026-05-13 — option (b) shipped; option (a) deferred behind prelude work]

- **ensure_owned_at_boundary migration — remaining specialized sites**: Core migration done. 5 remaining sites each have specialized logic beyond pure boundary-clones (fresh-string elision, last-use move, MutPtr wrapping, pattern extraction, field_access checks). Struct init was already covered. Enum variant init fixed (was missing `clone_multi_use_resource_args` at the `methods.rs` and `calls.rs` call sites — caused double-free on resource-typed fields in loops). [updated: 2026-04-16]

- **dict[key].push() index-mutate**: Prototype works for MutPtr in-place mutation. Needs `is_storing_method` flag on BuiltinMethodDecl. [updated: 2026-03-28]

- **`@[no_alloc]` function annotation**: Compiler error on allocating operations. [added: 2026-03-21]

- **Spawn captures don't check stale shared-derived**: Spawned closures can capture stale pre-await data. [added: 2026-03-18]

- **Replace auto-borrow with explicit reference semantics**: Phase 1 done (const_params). Phase 2 (const propagation) not started. [updated: 2026-03-20]

- **Collection Resource semantics: remaining call-site ownership gaps**: Borrow checker doesn't cover field assignment or method-call ownership transfer. [updated: 2026-03-22]

- **Drop elaboration — remaining cleanup**: (1) 24 Memsets across 17 fixtures remain: IndexLoad element zeroing (inside collection data arrays) and projected Deref/Field MoveZero (field-level ownership through pointers). Genuinely necessary — could be eliminated with element drop flags or `MoveField` instruction. (2) GIR still emits MoveZero for borrow-wrapped call args (field loads, MutPtr params), but these are zero-cost at runtime (V6 converts to MoveSlot). Removing the GIR emissions is code cleanliness, not a perf concern. [updated: 2026-04-14]

## Low

- **`xtd.regex` byte-mode opt-out (`utf8 = false` test cases).** With the 2026-05-20 codepoint-boundary fix in `lib/xtd/regex.gg`, empty-match iteration in `find_all`/`find_all_anchored`/`splitn`/`_replace_impl` now advances past UTF-8 codepoints (matches RE2 / rust-regex default `utf8 = true` semantics). Four conformance tests with explicit `utf8 = false` (`bytes/word-boundary-ascii-200`, `word-boundary/unicode5-not-only-ascii`, `regression/word-boundary-ascii-no-capture`, `regression/word-boundary-ascii-capture`) now legitimately fail because we don't expose an opt-in byte-mode counterpart. Two routes: (1) add `find_all_bytes` / `_replace_impl_bytes` variants that keep the old byte-level `pos = e + 1` semantics, and have the conformance runner dispatch by `utf8` flag; (2) leave it — byte mode is rarely useful for end-user code and these are 4/1184 tests. Route (1) is the right long-term call but blocked on whether anyone outside the conformance suite wants byte mode. Until then, the conformance runner should add a skip for `utf8 = false` non-ascii test cases (parent's job — runner sits in `gorget-conformance/`). [added: 2026-05-20]

- ~~**`resource_meta_for` is only valid post-population — populate earlier or document the constraint at the accessor.**~~ **DONE 2026-05-21** via path (b) — `resource_meta_for` lazily populates on cache miss (calls `build_resource_metadata` and memoises into `gmod.resource_metadata`). 20 consumer call sites flipped to the typed accessor. See item 6 above and DONE.md entry. [closed: 2026-05-21]

- **`for elem in vec1: vec2.push(elem)` fails IR validation where the equivalent indexed while-loop succeeds.** Surfaced 2026-05-19 during the lir_lower.gg/lower.gg while→for sweep (commit forthcoming). Concrete failing sites: `populate_drop_metadata` in `lir_lower.gg` (`field_drops.push(FieldDrop(finfo.name, ...))` inside `for finfo in info.fields:`), `lower_module`'s explicit-trait equip splitter (`own_methods.push(sm)` inside `for sm in eqblk.methods:`), `expand_meta_for_expr` (`expanded.push(ta)` inside `for ta in targs:`). Validator errors: `Tier 2a consume-site violation` or `read after MoveZero`. The for-loop appears to bind the iteration variable in a way the validator treats as borrow/move-zero state, but `Vector__T__push` is a CollectionMutator arg expecting owned. The while-loop's `vec.get(i).unwrap()` produces a value the validator can track as owned. **Two interpretations**, worth a design call before fixing: (a) **correct rejection** — for-loops should yield borrows by Phase D convention; pushing without an explicit `.clone()` is a genuine ownership violation the validator is rightly catching, and the agent's reverts were legitimate; (b) **gap** — for-loops should bind owned/cloneable values when the source `Vector[T]` is owned and the body consumes the element, and the validator is being over-conservative. Test by adding an explicit `.clone()` at one of the failing push sites and checking whether validation passes. If yes, it's interpretation (a) and the rule is "for-loops require explicit `.clone()` at consume sites" (worth documenting); if no, it's (b) and the validator needs the same loop-element-ownership tracking the while-loop benefits from. Estimate: half-day to test, plus a doc edit OR a validator fix. [added: 2026-05-19]

- **`gorget_string_copy_cow` 3-byte leak from f-string interpolating a String read through nested `Vector[Vector[String]]`.** Surfaced 2026-05-17 by the gorget_array_pop investigation (commit `4b3392a8`). Shape: `Vector[Vector[String]] outer = ...; print(f"{outer.pop().unwrap().get(0).unwrap()}")` leaks 3 bytes per evaluation. NOT a pop bug — the same leak reproduces without pop, e.g. `print(f"{outer.get(0).unwrap().get(0).unwrap()}")`. Localised to the f-string-interpolation lowering site: the implicit `gorget_string_copy_cow` (or similar) for the `{}` slot's String argument keeps the copy live past the f-string assembly, but the assembly path doesn't drop the temporary. Likely fix: trace `lower_fstring_interpolation` (or the AST→GIR rewrite for `EFString`) — confirm every interpolated expression's temporary is paired with a drop after the final concat. The 3-byte leak suggests one of the intermediate String COW headers (24-byte header + 3 bytes of "abc" payload that survives) — verify with LSan and grep COW emit sites for the missing drop. Estimate: half-day. [added: 2026-05-17, found while investigating the parser-claimed gorget_array_pop bug that didn't reproduce]

- **Clone reduction — 3 deferrable sites (audited 2026-05-16, kept)**: (1) `ensure_owned_at_boundary` struct-field init clone of Ptr(resource) (`context.rs:~1631`) → would need scope-escape check on the struct's lifetime, (2) Ptr-binding auto-clone at `lower_var_decl` (`stmts/mod.rs:~675`) → could defer to first mutation but needs mutation tracking across the no-clone span, (3) string field extraction in Constructor pattern (`stmts/patterns.rs:~937`) → needs per-arm escape analysis tracking returns / struct stores / captures. Each escape check is >30 lines of new logic at the consume site. Per CLAUDE.md ("fix complexity as signal of wrong layer"), the right fix is upstream — add typed escape metadata to the AST/GIR — that's a far bigger plumbing change than the marginal gain justifies. Audit of all 952 fixtures still showing max 5 implicit clones per fixture, all at necessary ownership boundaries. Audited and kept; re-evaluate if a future escape-analysis pass adds the typed metadata for free. [audited 2026-05-16; demoted from High 2026-04-09]

- **Self-host LIR backend**: ~6,200 lines across 4 files. 687/936 fixtures compile (was 462 baseline; net +225 over two sessions). 0 crashes. Key fixes across sessions: (1) SlotStore type-mismatch coercion — scalar→aggregate and aggregate→aggregate both emit `{0}` zero-init; (2) runtime fn return types — gorget_args/env_vars/cwd/str_to_upper/lower/char_at/byte_slice/int_to_str/float_to_str/bool_to_str all correctly typed; (3) runtime_arg_is_str table coerces pointers/scalars at Str parameter positions (str_cat/eq/cmp etc.); (4) ICmp narrowed to GorgetString plus memcmp fallback for struct==struct; (5) generic placeholder + enum variant filtering in type_defs; (6) bare opaque/prelude type constructors (TaskGroup, AtomicInt, Box, Shared, …); (7) is_type_constructor excludes primitive coercions; (8) post-gmod fn_sigs pass covers functions + equip methods; (9) extern time/time_ms/format_time/parse_time mappings; (10) Option/Result combinator takes address of aggregate src; (11) drop/clone forward declarations prevent static-after-implicit conflicts; (12) enum_variant_parent routes bare variant constructors to parent enum type; (13) Str/String/GorgetString identity coercion (Str("x") → x); (14) imported IEnum merged with __imported_type__ marker (skips drop/clone regen); (15) TFunction param ABI is Ptr(FnPtr) instead of unit — closure params now get pointer passing; (16) static method calls on type identifiers (Point.default(), int.parse(s)); (17) operator overload (+/-/*/div/rem/neg/==/!=/<=/>=) dispatches to TypeName__method for user structs, including monomorphized instances; (18) gorget_str_strip arity padding. Remaining ~249 failures: Str-as-int casts in JSON/XML/TOML parsers (b64_char_value), imported-struct field access (needs IStruct loader merge without drop conflicts — tried, regresses), DataFrame col_slice with Column placeholder types, Vector[T](alloc=…) keyword args, throws/Result auto-wrapping, SSA phi gaps (unassigned block params). [updated: 2026-04-17]

- **`meta is_pure(fn_name)` builtin**: Chicken-and-egg with pass ordering. [added: 2026-03-14]

- **`shared_stress_yield` flaky deadlock**: Timing-dependent contention. [added: 2026-03-11]

- **`shared static` support**: Thread-safe module-level statics. Workaround: explicit `Mutex[int]`. [added: 2026-03-10]

- **`Weak[T]` cycle-breaking — RESOLVED 2026-05-29 (superseded by arena/ECS).** `Cell[T]`/`RefCell[T]` were **rejected** (do not implement, do not mention); circular references are handled via the arena/ECS-style `std.slotmap` (`SlotMap[T]` + generational `SlotKey`, shipped 2026-05-25), and the `weak_cycle_*` fixtures were deleted. **REMAINING (doc-honesty cleanup, not a blocker):** `docs/book/16-smart-pointers.md:109-127` + `docs/language-reference.md §4.5` still describe the rejected `Cell`/`RefCell` + `Weak` cycle-breaking → clean up to point at the arena/ECS/`SlotMap` approach (devbook honesty-audit item #2). *Historical detail below (now stale):* Filed four ignored fixtures: `tests/fixtures/weak_cycle_shape_{a,b}_{positive,negative}.gg` documenting the canonical interior-mut and parent/child topologies, registered as `#[ignore]` in `tests/integration.rs` with expected stdout that reflects what the language *should* produce. The cycle-breaking claim in `docs/book/16-smart-pointers.md:109-127` and `docs/language-reference.md:529-531` is currently **untestable end-to-end** because every path to forming a real Shared cycle is blocked: **(1) `Cell[T]` is documented in the book as the interior-mutability primitive for Copy types, but it does not exist in the resolver** — `Cell[int] c = Cell[int](42)` fails with "undefined name `Cell`". Same for `RefCell[T]`. They are listed in the language reference (§4.5 Smart Pointer Types) but never reached the runtime. **(2) `Shared[T]` exposes no mutability path for arbitrary struct fields**. The only mutation through `Shared` that works today is `gorget_shared_array_set` (`Shared[Vector[T]].set_at`) — there is no `Shared.mutate(closure)`, no `Shared[Mutex[T]].lock()` returning a `Guard[T]` whose field-writes propagate (compiles, but `Guard__Struct field_assign` codegens broken C: `error: incompatible types when assigning to type 'Guard__T' from type 'int32_t'`), and `Guard[Vector[T]]` lacks auto-delegated `push`/`len` (undefined references at link time). **(3) `p.children.push(c)` for `p: Shared[Parent]` silently no-ops**. Compiles, runs, but the push targets a discarded copy of the inner struct; `p.get().children.len()` reads 0 after a sequence of pushes. No warning, no error, just data loss — this is the worst of the three because it gives the false impression that mutation through `Shared` works. **What needs to land**: any ONE of {`Cell[T]` runtime impl, `RefCell[T]` runtime impl, `Shared[Mutex[T]].lock()` codegen fix for `Guard[Struct]` field-assign, `Shared.mutate(closure)` API} unblocks at least one fixture shape. Until then, the book's cycle-breaking claim remains aspirational. The four ignored tests assert the canonical expected behaviour (`leaked=true` for the all-Shared control, `leaked=false` for the Weak-broken positive case) — when a primitive lands, removing the `#[ignore]` is the contract that the cycle-breaker actually breaks cycles. [updated: 2026-05-18, was added: 2026-05-17]

- **(Low, doc-honesty — discovered 2026-05-29 during devbook planning) `src/bir/mod.rs` header doc-comment is stale.** It claims *"Current state (Step 0): the scaffolding exists but no canonical ops have been added to LIR yet, so `lower_lir_to_bir` is a trivial passthrough and `assert_primitives_only` has an empty allowlist"* (`src/bir/mod.rs:20-26`). Reality: BIR is a shipped **6,253-line** subsystem — `lower_lir_to_bir` (`src/bir/lower.rs:41`, 4,058 lines) expands EnumInit/StructInit/TraitCall/CowClone/BoxAlloc + the full HofExpand family (Each/Any/All/Fold/Reduce/Count/Find/Filter); `assert_primitives_only` (`src/bir/validate.rs:36`) rejects 11 canonical ops (NOT empty); `synth.rs` (1,981 lines) generates sort/sort_by_key/trait-helper fns. Steps 3/4/5/7/9/10 + Commits 1-3 landed per DONE.md. Fix = rewrite the `mod.rs:20-26` "Current state" block — and the matching `src/bir/validate.rs:23-27` "accepts every LIR instruction" docstring (the validator actually has 11 rejection arms) — to describe shipped reality. **First finding of the devbook documentation-honesty audit (IMPL-AHEAD-OF-DOC direction).** [added 2026-05-29]

Next steps: scaffold README + per-chapter stubs (touches nothing in `internals/`), then per-chapter generation (adversarial accuracy pass + cross-doc honesty gate). **Open documentation-honesty findings to triage (implement vs cleanup) with the user:** #1 BIR header stale (logged above); #2 Cell/RefCell docs describe rejected primitives — `docs/book/16-smart-pointers.md:109-127` + `language-reference.md §4.5` (logged in the Weak entry); **#3** `language-design.md` restates ~3400/3561 lines of `language-reference.md`'s normative spec, violating the split `language-reference.md:5` declares → candidate: trim design.md to philosophy-only; **#4** numeric drift — Tier-3b proxy-read budget reads 77/64 in docs vs **78** in `tests/lints.rs:467`; CoW materialization count **6** (`feedback_cow_design_clarity.md`) vs **7** (the former `copy-on-write.md:215`, now folded into `docs/devbook/11-copy-on-write.md`).
